Piece 9: search, paging, rename, and delete on the server
Replace all of server/src/routes/students.js with this complete file. What's new is marked in the notes below it.

js
import { Router } from 'express';
import pool from '../db/pool.js';
import { generateStudentCode } from '../lib/studentCode.js';
import { normalizeFullName } from '../validation/student.js';

const router = Router();

const UNIQUE_VIOLATION = '23505';
const UUID_PATTERN =
  /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;
const DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE = 25;
const MAX_PAGE_SIZE = 100;

const STUDENT_COLUMNS = `
  id,
  student_code AS "studentCode",
  full_name    AS "fullName",
  created_at   AS "createdAt"
`;

function toPositiveInt(value, fallback) {
  const number = Number.parseInt(value, 10);
  return Number.isInteger(number) && number > 0 ? number : fallback;
}

function escapeLike(text) {
  return text.replace(/[\\%_]/g, '\\$&');
}

router.param('id', (req, res, next, value) => {
  if (!UUID_PATTERN.test(value)) {
    return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Student not found.' });
  }
  next();
});

router.post('/', async (req, res) => {
  const parsed = normalizeFullName(req.body?.fullName);
  if (parsed.error) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: parsed.error });
  }

  for (let attempt = 1; attempt <= 5; attempt++) {
    try {
      const result = await pool.query(
        `INSERT INTO students (student_code, full_name)
         VALUES ($1, $2)
         RETURNING ${STUDENT_COLUMNS}`,
        [generateStudentCode(), parsed.value]
      );
      return res.status(201).json(result.rows[0]);
    } catch (err) {
      if (err.code !== UNIQUE_VIOLATION) throw err;
    }
  }

  res.status(500).json({ error: 'Could not create a unique student code. Please try again.' });
});

router.get('/', async (req, res) => {
  const search =
    typeof req.query.search === 'string' ? req.query.search.trim() : '';
  if (search.length > 100) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Search text is too long.' });
  }

  const page = Math.min(toPositiveInt(req.query.page, 1), 1_000_000);
  const pageSize = Math.min(
    toPositiveInt(req.query.pageSize, DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE),
    MAX_PAGE_SIZE
  );

  const conditions = ['deleted_at IS NULL'];
  const values = [];

  if (search) {
    values.push(`%${escapeLike(search)}%`);
    let condition = `full_name ILIKE $${values.length}`;

    const codeText = search.replace(/[-\s]/g, '');
    if (codeText) {
      values.push(`%${escapeLike(codeText)}%`);
      condition += ` OR student_code ILIKE $${values.length}`;
    }
    conditions.push(`(${condition})`);
  }

  const where = conditions.join(' AND ');

  const countResult = await pool.query(
    `SELECT count(*)::int AS total FROM students WHERE ${where}`,
    values
  );

  const listResult = await pool.query(
    `SELECT ${STUDENT_COLUMNS}
     FROM students
     WHERE ${where}
     ORDER BY created_at DESC, id
     LIMIT $${values.length + 1} OFFSET $${values.length + 2}`,
    [...values, pageSize, (page - 1) * pageSize]
  );

  res.json({
    students: listResult.rows,
    total: countResult.rows[0].total,
    page,
    pageSize,
  });
});

router.patch('/:id', async (req, res) => {
  if (req.body && 'studentCode' in req.body) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Student codes cannot be changed.' });
  }

  const parsed = normalizeFullName(req.body?.fullName);
  if (parsed.error) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: parsed.error });
  }

  const result = await pool.query(
    `UPDATE students
     SET full_name = $1
     WHERE id = $2 AND deleted_at IS NULL
     RETURNING ${STUDENT_COLUMNS}`,
    [parsed.value, req.params.id]
  );

  if (result.rowCount === 0) {
    return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Student not found.' });
  }
  res.json(result.rows[0]);
});

router.delete('/:id', async (req, res) => {
  const result = await pool.query(
    `UPDATE students
     SET deleted_at = now()
     WHERE id = $1 AND deleted_at IS NULL`,
    [req.params.id]
  );

  if (result.rowCount === 0) {
    return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Student not found.' });
  }
  res.status(204).end();
});

export default router;
What changed, in plain terms

Search (inside the list route)

ILIKE means "matches this pattern, ignoring upper/lower case." The % on each side means "anything before or after," so %maria% finds every name containing "maria."
A search also checks the student code. Hyphens and spaces are stripped first, so typing K7M2-QX9 finds the code K7M2QX9A.
escapeLike handles the fact that % and _ are wildcards in a pattern. Without it, someone searching for % would match everyone. The function puts a backslash in front of them so they mean themselves.
The $1, $2 placeholders are built in a loop. The SQL text is only ever made of fixed pieces we wrote, and the user's text always travels separately as values. That keeps it safe from SQL injection.
The search is case-insensitive but not accent-insensitive, so "maria" won't find "María." We can improve that later if it matters.
Paging

LIMIT says how many rows to return, and OFFSET says how many to skip. Page 3 with 25 per page skips 50.
ORDER BY created_at DESC, id adds id as a tie-breaker, so the order never shuffles between pages.
A separate count(*) query gives total, so the page can later say "Page 2 of 5." The ::int makes the number arrive as a number, not text.
The page size defaults to 25 and is capped at 100. The page number is capped too, so nobody can request something absurd.
IDs in the address

router.param('id', ...) runs automatically before any route containing :id. If the ID isn't shaped like a UUID, we answer 404 right away, instead of letting the database complain and produce a confusing 500.
Rename (PATCH)

Only fullName can change. If the request includes studentCode, it's refused with a 400, because the code must never change.
rowCount tells us how many rows the update touched. Zero means the student doesn't exist or was deleted, so we answer 404.
Delete (DELETE)

It's a soft delete: it only fills in deleted_at. The row stays in the database, and the code stays reserved. The answer is 204 No Content, meaning "done, nothing to send back."
Test it
The server restarts by itself when you save. Make sure you have at least 3 active students (add a few from the page if not). Run the tests in the same terminal tab, because a shell variable is used.

1. Search and paging

curl -s "http://localhost:3000/api/students?search=%25"
curl -s "http://localhost:3000/api/students?pageSize=2&page=2"
The first should list only the Marias, with total matching, whatever the case you type.
The second searches for a literal %. It should return an empty students list and total: 0, not everyone.
The third should return 2 students, with "page":2,"pageSize":2 and the same total as the full list.

Piece 10: search, paging, rename, and delete in React
cd /data/projects/student-panel/client
A. A "wait until typing stops" helper

Create src/lib/useDebouncedValue.js:

js
import { useEffect, useState } from 'react';

export function useDebouncedValue(value, delayMs) {
  const [debounced, setDebounced] = useState(value);

  useEffect(() => {
    const timer = setTimeout(() => setDebounced(value), delayMs);
    return () => clearTimeout(timer);
  }, [value, delayMs]);

  return debounced;
}
Without this, every keystroke would send a request to the server. This helper waits until you pause typing for 300 ms. It's a custom hook, which is just a function whose name starts with use and that uses other hooks. Each new keystroke cancels the previous timer (the return () => clearTimeout(...) cleanup), so only the last one fires.

B. The API functions

Replace all of src/api/students.js with:

js
import { request } from './http.js';

export function listStudents({ search = '', page = 1, pageSize = 25 } = {}) {
  const params = new URLSearchParams({
    page: String(page),
    pageSize: String(pageSize),
  });
  if (search) params.set('search', search);
  return request(`/api/students?${params}`);
}

export function createStudent(fullName) {
  return request('/api/students', {
    method: 'POST',
    body: JSON.stringify({ fullName }),
  });
}

export function renameStudent(id, fullName) {
  return request(`/api/students/${id}`, {
    method: 'PATCH',
    body: JSON.stringify({ fullName }),
  });
}

export function deleteStudent(id) {
  return request(`/api/students/${id}`, { method: 'DELETE' });
}
listStudents now takes the search text and page. It returns the whole answer (students, total, page, pageSize) instead of just the array.
URLSearchParams builds the ?search=...&page=... part and safely encodes special characters, like a % or spaces.
C. One table row that manages itself

Create src/components/StudentRow.jsx:

jsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import { useMutation, useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { deleteStudent, renameStudent } from '../api/students.js';
import { formatDateTime, formatStudentCode } from '../lib/format.js';

function StudentRow({ student }) {
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
  const [isEditing, setIsEditing] = useState(false);
  const [draftName, setDraftName] = useState(student.fullName);

  function refreshList() {
    queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['students'] });
  }

  const rename = useMutation({
    mutationFn: (fullName) => renameStudent(student.id, fullName),
    onSuccess: () => {
      setIsEditing(false);
      refreshList();
    },
  });

  const remove = useMutation({
    mutationFn: () => deleteStudent(student.id),
    onSuccess: refreshList,
  });

  function startEditing() {
    setDraftName(student.fullName);
    rename.reset();
    setIsEditing(true);
  }

  function handleSave(event) {
    event.preventDefault();
    rename.mutate(draftName);
  }

  function handleDelete() {
    const code = formatStudentCode(student.studentCode);
    if (window.confirm(`Delete ${student.fullName} (${code})?`)) {
      remove.mutate();
    }
  }

  return (
    <tr>
      <td className="code">{formatStudentCode(student.studentCode)}</td>
      <td>
        {isEditing ? (
          <form onSubmit={handleSave} className="row">
            <input
              value={draftName}
              onChange={(event) => setDraftName(event.target.value)}
              aria-label={`New name for ${student.fullName}`}
              autoFocus
            />
            <button type="submit" disabled={rename.isPending}>
              Save
            </button>
            <button type="button" onClick={() => setIsEditing(false)}>
              Cancel
            </button>
          </form>
        ) : (
          student.fullName
        )}
        {isEditing && rename.isError && (
          <p className="error">{rename.error.message}</p>
        )}
        {remove.isError && <p className="error">{remove.error.message}</p>}
      </td>
      <td>{formatDateTime(student.createdAt)}</td>
      <td className="actions">
        <button type="button" onClick={startEditing} disabled={isEditing}>
          Rename
        </button>
        <button type="button" onClick={handleDelete} disabled={remove.isPending}>
          {remove.isPending ? 'Deleting...' : 'Delete'}
        </button>
      </td>
    </tr>
  );
}

export default StudentRow;
Every row has its own small memory: isEditing ("am I in rename mode?") and draftName (the text being typed). That's why the row is its own component. Editing one row doesn't affect the others.
student arrives as a prop, which is a component's input from its parent.
On success, refreshList() marks every cached list as out of date, and ['students'] matches all of them (any search or page). The table then reloads by itself.
The name check happens on the server. If it refuses, its message shows under the input.
window.confirm is the browser's built-in "OK / Cancel" box. It's plain, but it works. A proper styled dialog can come later.
D. The table with paging

Replace all of src/components/StudentsTable.jsx with:

jsx
import { useEffect } from 'react';
import { keepPreviousData, useQuery } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { listStudents } from '../api/students.js';
import StudentRow from './StudentRow.jsx';

const PAGE_SIZE = 25;

function StudentsTable({ search, page, onPageChange }) {
  const { data, isPending, isError, error, isPlaceholderData } = useQuery({
    queryKey: ['students', { search, page }],
    queryFn: () => listStudents({ search, page, pageSize: PAGE_SIZE }),
    placeholderData: keepPreviousData,
  });

  const totalPages = data
    ? Math.max(1, Math.ceil(data.total / data.pageSize))
    : 1;

  useEffect(() => {
    if (data && page > totalPages) onPageChange(totalPages);
  }, [data, page, totalPages, onPageChange]);

  if (isPending) return <p>Loading students...</p>;
  if (isError) return <p className="error">{error.message}</p>;
  if (data.total === 0) {
    return (
      <p>
        {search
          ? `No students match "${search}".`
          : 'No students yet. Add the first one above.'}
      </p>
    );
  }

  return (
    <>
      <table className={isPlaceholderData ? 'stale' : undefined}>
        <thead>
          <tr>
            <th>Code</th>
            <th>Full name</th>
            <th>Added</th>
            <th>Actions</th>
          </tr>
        </thead>
        <tbody>
          {data.students.map((student) => (
            <StudentRow key={student.id} student={student} />
          ))}
        </tbody>
      </table>

      <div className="pager">
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={() => onPageChange(page - 1)}
          disabled={page <= 1}
        >
          Previous
        </button>
        <span>
          Page {page} of {totalPages} ({data.total} students)
        </span>
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={() => onPageChange(page + 1)}
          disabled={page >= totalPages}
        >
          Next
        </button>
      </div>
    </>
  );
}

export default StudentsTable;
The query key is now ['students', { search, page }]. TanStack Query keeps a separate cache entry for each combination and fetches whenever the key changes. placeholderData: keepPreviousData keeps showing the old rows while the new ones load, which avoids flicker. The table dims slightly (the stale class) during that moment.
totalPages comes from the server's total divided by the page size, rounded up.
The useEffect handles one edge case: if you delete the last student on the last page, that page no longer exists, so it moves you back one page instead of showing an empty one.
The <>...</> is a fragment. It lets a component return two elements (the table and the pager) without an extra wrapper.
PAGE_SIZE is a constant so the size can be changed in one place.
E. The page: search box and shared state

Replace all of src/pages/StudentsPage.jsx with:

jsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import StudentForm from '../components/StudentForm.jsx';
import StudentsTable from '../components/StudentsTable.jsx';
import { useDebouncedValue } from '../lib/useDebouncedValue.js';

function StudentsPage() {
  const [searchText, setSearchText] = useState('');
  const [page, setPage] = useState(1);
  const search = useDebouncedValue(searchText.trim(), 300);

  function handleSearchChange(event) {
    setSearchText(event.target.value);
    setPage(1);
  }

  return (
    <main>
      <h1>Students</h1>
      <section>
        <h2>Add a student</h2>
        <StudentForm />
      </section>
      <section>
        <h2>All students</h2>
        <input
          type="search"
          className="search"
          value={searchText}
          onChange={handleSearchChange}
          placeholder="Search by name or code"
          aria-label="Search students"
        />
        <StudentsTable search={search} page={page} onPageChange={setPage} />
      </section>
    </main>
  );
}

export default StudentsPage;
The page owns searchText and page, because both the search box and the table need to know them. Whenever two components need the same information, it lives in their closest common parent and gets passed down as props. Typing in the box also jumps back to page 1.

With this, Step 1 of the design (students with random codes) is complete. Next is Step 2: parameters, periods, and weekly evaluations. I'd start with the period logic and its tests, the small calculation that everything else depends on.

go next while I do 10

This piece is server-only, so it won't disturb piece 10. Do it after you finish that one, since the server restarts on every file save.

Piece 11 is the foundation of Step 2: the calculation that turns dates into period numbers and back. If it's wrong, every score lands in the wrong week, so we test it before anything depends on it.

Piece 11: period logic, with tests
The rules from the design:

One setting, the anchor, holds the Saturday that starts Period 1.
Period n runs from anchor + (n−1) weeks, Saturday to Friday.
The current period comes from today's date.
A. The settings table

Create server/src/db/migrations/002_create_settings.sql:

sql
CREATE TABLE settings (
  key        text PRIMARY KEY,
  value      text NOT NULL,
  updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
This is a tiny table for app-wide settings: one row per setting, with a name (key) and a text value. Our first setting will be period_anchor_date. Apply it:

cd /data/projects/student-panel/server
npm run migrate
You should see Applied 002_create_settings.sql. Migration 001 is skipped, because it already ran.

B. The period calculations

Create server/src/lib/periods.js:

js
const MS_PER_DAY = 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
const DAYS_PER_PERIOD = 7;

function dayNumber(isoDate) {
  const [year, month, day] = isoDate.split('-').map(Number);
  return Date.UTC(year, month - 1, day) / MS_PER_DAY;
}

function isoDateFromDayNumber(days) {
  return new Date(days * MS_PER_DAY).toISOString().slice(0, 10);
}

export function isValidIsoDate(text) {
  if (typeof text !== 'string' || !/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(text)) {
    return false;
  }
  return isoDateFromDayNumber(dayNumber(text)) === text;
}

export function isSaturday(isoDate) {
  return new Date(dayNumber(isoDate) * MS_PER_DAY).getUTCDay() === 6;
}

export function todayIso(now = new Date()) {
  const year = now.getFullYear();
  const month = String(now.getMonth() + 1).padStart(2, '0');
  const day = String(now.getDate()).padStart(2, '0');
  return `${year}-${month}-${day}`;
}

export function periodForDate(anchorDate, date) {
  const daysSinceAnchor = dayNumber(date) - dayNumber(anchorDate);
  if (daysSinceAnchor < 0) return null;
  return Math.floor(daysSinceAnchor / DAYS_PER_PERIOD) + 1;
}

export function periodDates(anchorDate, number) {
  if (!Number.isInteger(number) || number < 1) {
    throw new Error('Period number must be a whole number from 1.');
  }
  const start = dayNumber(anchorDate) + (number - 1) * DAYS_PER_PERIOD;
  return {
    number,
    startDate: isoDateFromDayNumber(start),
    endDate: isoDateFromDayNumber(start + DAYS_PER_PERIOD - 1),
  };
}
How it works:

All dates are plain text like 2026-09-19 (year-month-day, no time of day).
To do arithmetic, each date is converted to a day number, meaning whole days since 1 Jan 1970, counted in UTC. Adding 7 days is then exact, and daylight-saving changes or time zones can't shift a period by an hour or a day.
periodForDate is the heart of it: days since the anchor, divided by 7 and rounded down, plus 1. Day 0–6 gives Period 1, day 7–13 gives Period 2, and so on. A date before the anchor gives null, meaning "before Period 1."
periodDates runs it backwards: the start is the anchor plus (n−1) weeks, and the end is 6 days later.
isValidIsoDate rejects impossible dates like 2026-02-31. It converts to a day number and back, and a date that "rolls over" into March no longer matches.
todayIso reads the computer's own calendar day and time zone, so a period changes at local midnight between Friday and Saturday.
C. Automated tests

Instead of testing by hand, we write a file that checks the functions against answers we know are right. Create server/test/periods.test.js:

js
import { test } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import {
  isSaturday,
  isValidIsoDate,
  periodDates,
  periodForDate,
  todayIso,
} from '../src/lib/periods.js';

const ANCHOR = '2026-09-19'; // a Saturday

test('recognises valid dates and Saturdays', () => {
  assert.equal(isValidIsoDate('2026-09-19'), true);
  assert.equal(isValidIsoDate('2026-02-31'), false);
  assert.equal(isValidIsoDate('2026-9-19'), false);
  assert.equal(isSaturday('2026-09-19'), true);
  assert.equal(isSaturday('2026-09-20'), false);
});

test('period 1 runs from the anchor Saturday to the following Friday', () => {
  assert.equal(periodForDate(ANCHOR, '2026-09-19'), 1);
  assert.equal(periodForDate(ANCHOR, '2026-09-25'), 1);
});

test('the next Saturday starts period 2', () => {
  assert.equal(periodForDate(ANCHOR, '2026-09-26'), 2);
});

test('dates before the anchor belong to no period', () => {
  assert.equal(periodForDate(ANCHOR, '2026-09-18'), null);
});

test('later dates, including across a daylight-saving change', () => {
  assert.equal(periodForDate(ANCHOR, '2026-10-30'), 6);
  assert.equal(periodForDate(ANCHOR, '2026-10-31'), 7);
  assert.equal(periodForDate(ANCHOR, '2026-12-31'), 15);
});

test('period dates', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(periodDates(ANCHOR, 1), {
    number: 1,
    startDate: '2026-09-19',
    endDate: '2026-09-25',
  });
  assert.deepEqual(periodDates(ANCHOR, 2), {
    number: 2,
    startDate: '2026-09-26',
    endDate: '2026-10-02',
  });
  assert.deepEqual(periodDates(ANCHOR, 15), {
    number: 15,
    startDate: '2026-12-26',
    endDate: '2027-01-01',
  });
});

test('period numbers must be whole numbers from 1', () => {
  assert.throws(() => periodDates(ANCHOR, 0));
  assert.throws(() => periodDates(ANCHOR, 1.5));
});

test("today's date uses the computer's local calendar day", () => {
  assert.equal(todayIso(new Date(2026, 8, 20, 23, 59)), '2026-09-20');
  assert.equal(todayIso(new Date(2026, 8, 20, 0, 1)), '2026-09-20');
});

test('the period changes at local midnight between Friday and Saturday', () => {
  assert.equal(periodForDate(ANCHOR, todayIso(new Date(2026, 8, 25, 23, 59))), 1);
  assert.equal(periodForDate(ANCHOR, todayIso(new Date(2026, 8, 26, 0, 1))), 2);
});
Each test(...) names a situation, and each assert.equal(actual, expected) fails loudly if the function's answer differs. node:test is built into Node, so nothing extra to install. (In JavaScript dates, months count from 0, so new Date(2026, 8, ...) is September.)

Add a script and run it:

npm pkg set scripts.test="node --test"
npm test
You should see a green check for each test and a summary ending in pass 9 and fail 0. If anything fails, send me the output before continuing, because everything else depends on it.

D. The routes

Create server/src/routes/periods.js:

js
import { Router } from 'express';
import pool from '../db/pool.js';
import {
  isSaturday,
  isValidIsoDate,
  periodDates,
  periodForDate,
  todayIso,
} from '../lib/periods.js';

const router = Router();
const ANCHOR_KEY = 'period_anchor_date';

async function readAnchor() {
  const result = await pool.query(
    'SELECT value FROM settings WHERE key = $1',
    [ANCHOR_KEY]
  );
  return result.rows[0]?.value ?? null;
}

router.get('/settings/period-anchor', async (req, res) => {
  res.json({ anchorDate: await readAnchor() });
});

router.put('/settings/period-anchor', async (req, res) => {
  const anchorDate = req.body?.anchorDate;
  if (!isValidIsoDate(anchorDate)) {
    return res
      .status(400)
      .json({ error: 'anchorDate must be a real date written as YYYY-MM-DD.' });
  }
  if (!isSaturday(anchorDate)) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'The anchor date must be a Saturday.' });
  }

  await pool.query(
    `INSERT INTO settings (key, value)
     VALUES ($1, $2)
     ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE
       SET value = EXCLUDED.value, updated_at = now()`,
    [ANCHOR_KEY, anchorDate]
  );
  res.json({ anchorDate });
});

router.get('/periods', async (req, res) => {
  const today = todayIso();
  const anchorDate = await readAnchor();
  if (!anchorDate) {
    return res.json({ anchorDate: null, today, current: null, periods: [] });
  }

  const current = periodForDate(anchorDate, today);
  const periods = [];
  for (let number = 1; number <= (current ?? 0); number++) {
    periods.push(periodDates(anchorDate, number));
  }
  res.json({ anchorDate, today, current, periods });
});

export default router;
PUT validates the date, then saves it with an "upsert": INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE means "add the row, or update it if the setting already exists."
GET /api/periods answers with the anchor, today's date, the current period number, and the list of periods from 1 up to the current one (with dates).
Connect it in server/src/index.js. Add this import next to the others at the top:

js
import periodsRouter from './routes/periods.js';
And this line right after app.use('/api/students', studentsRouter);:

js
app.use('/api', periodsRouter);
One rule for later: the anchor can't change once scores exist. No scores can exist yet, so changing it is safe right now. We'll add the lock check when we build the scores.

go next while I do 11

Piece 12 is server-only, and it needs piece 11 finished first (the migrations run in order). It adds the list of parameters (Speed, Rhythm, ...) and the change log that decides which parameters each period uses. This is the rule from the design, so we test it heavily.

Piece 12: parameters and per-period rules
A. The tables

Create server/src/db/migrations/003_create_parameters.sql:

sql
CREATE TABLE parameters (
  id         uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  name       text NOT NULL,
  unit       text,
  min_value  numeric,
  max_value  numeric,
  sort_order integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  CONSTRAINT parameter_name_length
    CHECK (char_length(btrim(name)) BETWEEN 1 AND 60),
  CONSTRAINT parameter_range_order
    CHECK (min_value IS NULL OR max_value IS NULL OR min_value <= max_value)
);

CREATE UNIQUE INDEX parameters_name_unique ON parameters (lower(name));

CREATE TABLE parameter_changes (
  id            uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  parameter_id  uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES parameters (id),
  period_number integer NOT NULL,
  scope         text NOT NULL,
  action        text NOT NULL,
  created_at    timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  CONSTRAINT change_period_positive CHECK (period_number >= 1),
  CONSTRAINT change_scope_valid
    CHECK (scope IN ('from_here_on', 'this_period_only')),
  CONSTRAINT change_action_valid CHECK (action IN ('add', 'remove')),
  UNIQUE (parameter_id, period_number, scope)
);
parameters is the catalog: what can be measured. unit, min_value, and max_value are optional, so a parameter can be a score (0–10) or a measurement (km/h). numeric is PostgreSQL's exact-number type.
The unique index on lower(name) makes names unique regardless of case, so "Speed" and "speed" can't both exist.
parameter_changes is the log from the design. REFERENCES parameters (id) is a foreign key: a change can only point at a parameter that exists.
The CHECK ... IN (...) rules limit scope and action to the four allowed combinations.
UNIQUE (parameter_id, period_number, scope) means one change per parameter, period, and scope.
There's no delete for parameters, on purpose. Removing one from use is done through the log.
Apply it:

cd /data/projects/student-panel/server
npm run migrate
You should see Applied 003_create_parameters.sql.

B. The rule, as one small function

Create src/lib/parameterUse.js:

js
export const MAX_PERIOD = 5000;

export function isValidPeriodNumber(value) {
  return Number.isInteger(value) && value >= 1 && value <= MAX_PERIOD;
}

function isUsed(changesForOneParameter, periodNumber) {
  let used = false;
  let latestStart = 0;

  for (const change of changesForOneParameter) {
    if (
      change.scope === 'from_here_on' &&
      change.periodNumber <= periodNumber &&
      change.periodNumber > latestStart
    ) {
      latestStart = change.periodNumber;
      used = change.action === 'add';
    }
  }

  const oneOff = changesForOneParameter.find(
    (change) =>
      change.scope === 'this_period_only' && change.periodNumber === periodNumber
  );
  if (oneOff) used = oneOff.action === 'add';

  return used;
}

export function parametersUsedInPeriod(changes, periodNumber) {
  const byParameter = new Map();
  for (const change of changes) {
    if (!byParameter.has(change.parameterId)) {
      byParameter.set(change.parameterId, []);
    }
    byParameter.get(change.parameterId).push(change);
  }

  const usedIds = [];
  for (const [parameterId, list] of byParameter) {
    if (isUsed(list, periodNumber)) usedIds.push(parameterId);
  }
  return usedIds;
}
isUsed is the rule for one parameter. It finds the latest "from here on" change at or before the period (the last one wins), and that decides the starting answer. Then, if a "this period only" change exists for exactly this period, it overrides.
parametersUsedInPeriod groups all the changes by parameter and returns the IDs of those in use. A parameter with no changes never appears, so it's used nowhere.
C. Tests

Create test/parameterUse.test.js:

js
import { test } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import {
  MAX_PERIOD,
  isValidPeriodNumber,
  parametersUsedInPeriod,
} from '../src/lib/parameterUse.js';

const changes = [
  { parameterId: 'speed', periodNumber: 1, scope: 'from_here_on', action: 'add' },
  { parameterId: 'rhythm', periodNumber: 1, scope: 'from_here_on', action: 'add' },
  { parameterId: 'rhythm', periodNumber: 10, scope: 'from_here_on', action: 'remove' },
  { parameterId: 'balance', periodNumber: 5, scope: 'from_here_on', action: 'add' },
  { parameterId: 'balance', periodNumber: 7, scope: 'this_period_only', action: 'remove' },
];

const ids = (log, n) => parametersUsedInPeriod(log, n).sort();

test('the example log gives the expected parameters for each period', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(ids(changes, 1), ['rhythm', 'speed']);
  assert.deepEqual(ids(changes, 4), ['rhythm', 'speed']);
  assert.deepEqual(ids(changes, 5), ['balance', 'rhythm', 'speed']);
  assert.deepEqual(ids(changes, 6), ['balance', 'rhythm', 'speed']);
  assert.deepEqual(ids(changes, 7), ['rhythm', 'speed']);
  assert.deepEqual(ids(changes, 8), ['balance', 'rhythm', 'speed']);
  assert.deepEqual(ids(changes, 9), ['balance', 'rhythm', 'speed']);
  assert.deepEqual(ids(changes, 10), ['balance', 'speed']);
  assert.deepEqual(ids(changes, 50), ['balance', 'speed']);
});

test('a parameter can be dropped and later added back', () => {
  const log = [
    ...changes,
    { parameterId: 'rhythm', periodNumber: 14, scope: 'from_here_on', action: 'add' },
  ];
  assert.deepEqual(ids(log, 13), ['balance', 'speed']);
  assert.deepEqual(ids(log, 14), ['balance', 'rhythm', 'speed']);
});

test('a one-period addition applies to that period only', () => {
  const log = [
    { parameterId: 'extra', periodNumber: 3, scope: 'this_period_only', action: 'add' },
  ];
  assert.deepEqual(ids(log, 2), []);
  assert.deepEqual(ids(log, 3), ['extra']);
  assert.deepEqual(ids(log, 4), []);
});

test('a one-period addition works inside a removed stretch', () => {
  const log = [
    { parameterId: 'p', periodNumber: 1, scope: 'from_here_on', action: 'add' },
    { parameterId: 'p', periodNumber: 10, scope: 'from_here_on', action: 'remove' },
    { parameterId: 'p', periodNumber: 12, scope: 'this_period_only', action: 'add' },
  ];
  assert.deepEqual(ids(log, 11), []);
  assert.deepEqual(ids(log, 12), ['p']);
  assert.deepEqual(ids(log, 13), []);
});

test('a change planned for a future period does not affect earlier ones', () => {
  const log = [
    { parameterId: 'p', periodNumber: 20, scope: 'from_here_on', action: 'add' },
  ];
  assert.deepEqual(ids(log, 19), []);
  assert.deepEqual(ids(log, 20), ['p']);
});

test('with no changes, nothing is used', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(ids([], 1), []);
});

test('period numbers must be whole numbers within range', () => {
  assert.equal(isValidPeriodNumber(1), true);
  assert.equal(isValidPeriodNumber(MAX_PERIOD), true);
  assert.equal(isValidPeriodNumber(0), false);
  assert.equal(isValidPeriodNumber(1.5), false);
  assert.equal(isValidPeriodNumber(MAX_PERIOD + 1), false);
  assert.equal(isValidPeriodNumber('3'), false);
});
The first test is the exact example from the design (Speed, Rhythm, and Balance). Run everything:

npm test
You should see pass 16 and fail 0 (9 from piece 11, 7 new). If any fail, send me the output before continuing.

D. Checking the input

Create src/validation/parameter.js:

js
const MAX_NAME = 60;
const MAX_UNIT = 20;

export function parseParameterInput(body, { partial = false } = {}) {
  const input = body ?? {};
  const values = {};

  if (!partial || 'name' in input) {
    const name =
      typeof input.name === 'string'
        ? input.name.trim().replace(/\s+/g, ' ')
        : '';
    if (name.length < 1 || name.length > MAX_NAME) {
      return { error: `Name must be between 1 and ${MAX_NAME} characters.` };
    }
    values.name = name;
  }

  if ('unit' in input) {
    if (input.unit === null || input.unit === '') {
      values.unit = null;
    } else if (
      typeof input.unit === 'string' &&
      input.unit.trim().length <= MAX_UNIT
    ) {
      values.unit = input.unit.trim();
    } else {
      return { error: `Unit must be text of at most ${MAX_UNIT} characters.` };
    }
  }

  for (const key of ['minValue', 'maxValue']) {
    if (key in input) {
      if (input[key] === null) {
        values[key] = null;
      } else if (typeof input[key] === 'number' && Number.isFinite(input[key])) {
        values[key] = input[key];
      } else {
        return { error: `${key} must be a number or null.` };
      }
    }
  }
  if (
    values.minValue != null &&
    values.maxValue != null &&
    values.minValue > values.maxValue
  ) {
    return { error: 'The minimum cannot be larger than the maximum.' };
  }

  if ('sortOrder' in input) {
    if (!Number.isInteger(input.sortOrder)) {
      return { error: 'sortOrder must be a whole number.' };
    }
    values.sortOrder = input.sortOrder;
  }

  return { values };
}
The same function serves two cases. For creating (partial: false) a name is required. For editing (partial: true) only the fields you send are checked and returned, so you can change one thing without touching the rest. Sending null for unit, minValue, or maxValue clears it.

E. The routes

Create src/routes/parameters.js:

js
import { Router } from 'express';
import pool from '../db/pool.js';
import { parseParameterInput } from '../validation/parameter.js';
import {
  MAX_PERIOD,
  isValidPeriodNumber,
  parametersUsedInPeriod,
} from '../lib/parameterUse.js';

const router = Router();

const UNIQUE_VIOLATION = '23505';
const CHECK_VIOLATION = '23514';
const FOREIGN_KEY_VIOLATION = '23503';
const UUID_PATTERN =
  /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;
const SCOPES = ['from_here_on', 'this_period_only'];
const ACTIONS = ['add', 'remove'];

const PARAMETER_COLUMNS = `
  id,
  name,
  unit,
  min_value::float8 AS "minValue",
  max_value::float8 AS "maxValue",
  sort_order        AS "sortOrder"
`;

const CHANGE_COLUMNS = `
  id,
  parameter_id  AS "parameterId",
  period_number AS "periodNumber",
  scope,
  action,
  created_at    AS "createdAt"
`;

router.param('id', (req, res, next, value) => {
  if (!UUID_PATTERN.test(value)) {
    return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Not found.' });
  }
  next();
});

router.param('number', (req, res, next, value) => {
  if (!/^\d+$/.test(value) || !isValidPeriodNumber(Number(value))) {
    return res.status(400).json({
      error: `Period must be a whole number from 1 to ${MAX_PERIOD}.`,
    });
  }
  next();
});

function handleParameterError(err, res) {
  if (err.code === UNIQUE_VIOLATION) {
    res.status(409).json({ error: 'A parameter with that name already exists.' });
    return true;
  }
  if (err.code === CHECK_VIOLATION) {
    res
      .status(400)
      .json({ error: 'The minimum cannot be larger than the maximum.' });
    return true;
  }
  return false;
}

router.get('/parameters', async (req, res) => {
  const result = await pool.query(
    `SELECT ${PARAMETER_COLUMNS}
     FROM parameters
     ORDER BY sort_order, lower(name)`
  );
  res.json({ parameters: result.rows });
});

router.post('/parameters', async (req, res) => {
  const parsed = parseParameterInput(req.body);
  if (parsed.error) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: parsed.error });
  }

  const startPeriod = req.body?.startPeriod;
  if (!isValidPeriodNumber(startPeriod)) {
    return res.status(400).json({
      error: `startPeriod must be a whole number from 1 to ${MAX_PERIOD}.`,
    });
  }

  const { name, unit = null, minValue = null, maxValue = null } = parsed.values;

  const client = await pool.connect();
  try {
    await client.query('BEGIN');
    const created = await client.query(
      `INSERT INTO parameters (name, unit, min_value, max_value, sort_order)
       VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4,
               (SELECT COALESCE(MAX(sort_order), 0) + 1 FROM parameters))
       RETURNING ${PARAMETER_COLUMNS}`,
      [name, unit, minValue, maxValue]
    );
    await client.query(
      `INSERT INTO parameter_changes (parameter_id, period_number, scope, action)
       VALUES ($1, $2, 'from_here_on', 'add')`,
      [created.rows[0].id, startPeriod]
    );
    await client.query('COMMIT');
    res.status(201).json(created.rows[0]);
  } catch (err) {
    await client.query('ROLLBACK');
    if (handleParameterError(err, res)) return;
    throw err;
  } finally {
    client.release();
  }
});

router.patch('/parameters/:id', async (req, res) => {
  const parsed = parseParameterInput(req.body, { partial: true });
  if (parsed.error) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: parsed.error });
  }

  const columns = {
    name: 'name',
    unit: 'unit',
    minValue: 'min_value',
    maxValue: 'max_value',
    sortOrder: 'sort_order',
  };
  const entries = Object.entries(parsed.values);
  if (entries.length === 0) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Nothing to change.' });
  }

  const assignments = entries.map(
    ([key], index) => `${columns[key]} = $${index + 1}`
  );
  const values = entries.map(([, value]) => value);

  try {
    const result = await pool.query(
      `UPDATE parameters
       SET ${assignments.join(', ')}
       WHERE id = $${values.length + 1}
       RETURNING ${PARAMETER_COLUMNS}`,
      [...values, req.params.id]
    );
    if (result.rowCount === 0) {
      return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Parameter not found.' });
    }
    res.json(result.rows[0]);
  } catch (err) {
    if (handleParameterError(err, res)) return;
    throw err;
  }
});

router.get('/parameter-changes', async (req, res) => {
  const result = await pool.query(
    `SELECT c.id,
            c.parameter_id  AS "parameterId",
            p.name          AS "parameterName",
            c.period_number AS "periodNumber",
            c.scope,
            c.action,
            c.created_at    AS "createdAt"
     FROM parameter_changes c
     JOIN parameters p ON p.id = c.parameter_id
     ORDER BY c.period_number, p.sort_order, c.scope`
  );
  res.json({ changes: result.rows });
});

router.post('/parameter-changes', async (req, res) => {
  const { parameterId, periodNumber, scope, action } = req.body ?? {};

  if (typeof parameterId !== 'string' || !UUID_PATTERN.test(parameterId)) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'parameterId must be a valid ID.' });
  }
  if (!isValidPeriodNumber(periodNumber)) {
    return res.status(400).json({
      error: `periodNumber must be a whole number from 1 to ${MAX_PERIOD}.`,
    });
  }
  if (!SCOPES.includes(scope)) {
    return res
      .status(400)
      .json({ error: `scope must be one of: ${SCOPES.join(', ')}.` });
  }
  if (!ACTIONS.includes(action)) {
    return res
      .status(400)
      .json({ error: `action must be one of: ${ACTIONS.join(', ')}.` });
  }

  try {
    const result = await pool.query(
      `INSERT INTO parameter_changes (parameter_id, period_number, scope, action)
       VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4)
       ON CONFLICT (parameter_id, period_number, scope)
       DO UPDATE SET action = EXCLUDED.action, created_at = now()
       RETURNING ${CHANGE_COLUMNS}`,
      [parameterId, periodNumber, scope, action]
    );
    res.status(201).json(result.rows[0]);
  } catch (err) {
    if (err.code === FOREIGN_KEY_VIOLATION) {
      return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Parameter not found.' });
    }
    throw err;
  }
});

router.delete('/parameter-changes/:id', async (req, res) => {
  const result = await pool.query(
    'DELETE FROM parameter_changes WHERE id = $1',
    [req.params.id]
  );
  if (result.rowCount === 0) {
    return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Change not found.' });
  }
  res.status(204).end();
});

router.get('/periods/:number/parameters', async (req, res) => {
  const periodNumber = Number(req.params.number);

  const [changesResult, parametersResult] = await Promise.all([
    pool.query(
      `SELECT parameter_id  AS "parameterId",
              period_number AS "periodNumber",
              scope,
              action
       FROM parameter_changes`
    ),
    pool.query(
      `SELECT ${PARAMETER_COLUMNS}
       FROM parameters
       ORDER BY sort_order, lower(name)`
    ),
  ]);

  const usedIds = new Set(parametersUsedInPeriod(changesResult.rows, periodNumber));
  res.json({
    periodNumber,
    parameters: parametersResult.rows.filter((parameter) =>
      usedIds.has(parameter.id)
    ),
  });
});

export default router;
What's worth knowing:

min_value::float8 converts the exact numeric type into a regular number. Without it, the pg library hands back "10" as text, which causes subtle bugs later in React.
Creating a parameter inserts the parameter and its first "add from period X on" change inside one transaction. Both happen or neither does, so a parameter never exists without a start.
Editing builds the SET part from a fixed list of column names we wrote, and the values travel separately as placeholders, so it's safe.
Adding a change is an upsert. Posting the same parameter, period, and scope again replaces the old action (the "a new one for the same scope replaces the old" rule).
Deleting a change is the "undo." No scores exist yet, so nothing can be hidden by it. When scores exist, changes will warn you first.
router.param('number') checks the period number in the address before any route with :number runs.
The last route loads all changes and all parameters, runs our tested function, and keeps only the parameters in use. It's what the Evaluate grid will call.
Range editing will be locked once scores exist, from the scores piece onward.
Connect it in src/index.js. Add this import with the others:

js
import parametersRouter from './routes/parameters.js';
And this line right after app.use('/api', periodsRouter);:

js
app.use('/api', parametersRouter);
F. Try it

The server restarts on save. First define a small helper that pulls the id out of an answer. Then create the three parameters from the design example, in the same terminal tab:

getid() { node -e "let s='';process.stdin.on('data',d=>s+=d).on('end',()=>console.log(JSON.parse(s).id))"; }
SPEED=$(curl -s -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/parameters -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{"name":"Speed","minValue":0,"maxValue":10,"startPeriod":1}' | getid)
RHYTHM=$(curl -s -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/parameters -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{"name":"Rhythm","minValue":0,"maxValue":10,"startPeriod":1}' | getid)
BALANCE=$(curl -s -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/parameters -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{"name":"Balance","minValue":0,"maxValue":10,"startPeriod":5}' | getid)
echo $SPEED $RHYTHM $BALANCE
echo should print three long IDs. Now add the two other changes from the example:

curl -s -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/parameter-changes -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d "{\"parameterId\":\"$RHYTHM\",\"periodNumber\":10,\"scope\":\"from_here_on\",\"action\":\"remove\"}"
curl -s -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/parameter-changes -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d "{\"parameterId\":\"$BALANCE\",\"periodNumber\":7,\"scope\":\"this_period_only\",\"action\":\"remove\"}"
Each should answer with the saved change. Then ask which parameters each period uses:

for n in 3 5 7 8 10; do echo "Period $n:"; curl -s http://localhost:3000/api/periods/$n/parameters | node -e "let s='';process.stdin.on('data',d=>s+=d).on('end',()=>console.log(JSON.parse(s).parameters.map(p=>p.name).join(', ')))"; done
Expected:

curl -s -w "\nHTTP %{http_code}\n" -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/parameter-changes -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d "{\"parameterId\":\"$BALANCE\",\"periodNumber\":9,\"scope\":\"sometimes\",\"action\":\"add\"}"

HTTP 400, scope must be one of the allowed values

CHANGE=$(curl -s http://localhost:3000/api/parameter-changes | node -e "let s='';process.stdin.on('data',d=>s+=d).on('end',()=>console.log(JSON.parse(s).changes.find(c=>c.scope==='this_period_only').id))")

That line was unclear. The "step 4" I meant is the 4th command in the list of seven curl checks in step E (the PUT with YOUR_SATURDAY), not a lettered step.

What happened. The 6th command set the anchor to 2030-01-05, a Saturday in the future. That was only to test the "this period hasn't started yet" case. But the anchor is saved in the database, so right now the app believes Period 1 starts in 2030. It reports no current period and an empty list. Later pieces (saving scores, the Evaluate page) need a real current period, so the anchor has to go back to a past Saturday.

The date will be your Saturday. If it shows 2030-01-05, the reset didn't work, so run the command in step 1 again.

Piece 13: scores (evaluations)
This piece adds the table for scores, plus routes to save and load them. It also switches on two locks we promised:

The anchor date can't change once scores exist.
A parameter's range can't change once it has scores.
One change from the blueprint: clearing a score will keep its row and empty its value, instead of deleting the row. That way, when we add the 7-day/1-day locking later, someone can't clear a score and retype it to restart the clock. Loading scores will simply ignore empty values.

A. The table

Create server/src/db/migrations/004_create_evaluations.sql:

sql
CREATE TABLE evaluations (
  id            uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  student_id    uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES students (id),
  parameter_id  uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES parameters (id),
  period_number integer NOT NULL,
  value         numeric,
  created_at    timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  updated_at    timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  CONSTRAINT evaluation_period_positive CHECK (period_number >= 1),
  UNIQUE (student_id, parameter_id, period_number)
);

CREATE INDEX evaluations_period_idx ON evaluations (period_number);
CREATE INDEX evaluations_parameter_idx ON evaluations (parameter_id);
Each row is one score: one student, one parameter, one period. UNIQUE (student_id, parameter_id, period_number) allows only one score per combination.
value can be empty (NULL), which means "cleared".
The UNIQUE rule already makes lookups by student fast. The two extra indexes (like a book's index) speed up "everything in one period" and "everything for one parameter".
cd /data/projects/student-panel/server
npm run migrate
You should see Applied 004_create_evaluations.sql.

B. The checking logic, with tests

The risky part of saving is deciding which entries are valid. We put that in plain functions that don't touch the database, so we can test them.

Create src/lib/ids.js:

js
const UUID_PATTERN =
  /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;

export function isUuid(value) {
  return typeof value === 'string' && UUID_PATTERN.test(value);
}
Create src/lib/evaluationCheck.js:

js
import { isUuid } from './ids.js';

export const MAX_ENTRIES = 2000;
const MAX_ABS_VALUE = 1_000_000_000;

export function validateShape(entries) {
  if (!Array.isArray(entries) || entries.length === 0) {
    return 'entries must be a non-empty list.';
  }
  if (entries.length > MAX_ENTRIES) {
    return `Too many entries (the maximum is ${MAX_ENTRIES}).`;
  }

  for (const entry of entries) {
    if (!isUuid(entry?.studentId)) {
      return 'Every entry needs a valid studentId.';
    }
    if (!isUuid(entry.parameterId)) {
      return 'Every entry needs a valid parameterId.';
    }

    const { value } = entry;
    const isNumber = typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value);
    if (value !== null && !isNumber) {
      return 'Every value must be a number or null.';
    }
    if (isNumber && Math.abs(value) > MAX_ABS_VALUE) {
      return 'A value is outside the supported range.';
    }
  }
  return null;
}

export function findProblems({
  entries,
  usedParameters,
  knownStudentIds,
  periodNumber,
}) {
  const problems = [];
  const seen = new Set();

  entries.forEach((entry, index) => {
    function report(message) {
      problems.push({
        index,
        studentId: entry.studentId,
        parameterId: entry.parameterId,
        message,
      });
    }

    const key = `${entry.studentId}:${entry.parameterId}`;
    if (seen.has(key)) {
      report('This student and parameter appear more than once.');
      return;
    }
    seen.add(key);

    if (!knownStudentIds.has(entry.studentId)) {
      report('Student not found.');
      return;
    }

    const parameter = usedParameters.get(entry.parameterId);
    if (!parameter) {
      report(`This parameter is not used in Period ${periodNumber}.`);
      return;
    }

    if (entry.value === null) return;

    if (parameter.minValue !== null && entry.value < parameter.minValue) {
      report(`${parameter.name} must be at least ${parameter.minValue}.`);
    } else if (parameter.maxValue !== null && entry.value > parameter.maxValue) {
      report(`${parameter.name} must be at most ${parameter.maxValue}.`);
    }
  });

  return problems;
}
validateShape checks the request's form: a real list, real IDs, and a value that is a number or an explicit null. It returns a message, or null if all is fine.
findProblems checks the meaning of each entry: duplicates, an unknown student, a parameter this period doesn't use, or a value outside the parameter's range. It collects every problem with its position (index) so the screen can mark the right cell.
Create test/evaluationCheck.test.js:

js
import { test } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { findProblems, validateShape } from '../src/lib/evaluationCheck.js';

const S1 = '11111111-1111-4111-8111-111111111111';
const S2 = '22222222-2222-4222-8222-222222222222';
const SPEED = 'aaaaaaaa-aaaa-4aaa-8aaa-aaaaaaaaaaaa';
const OPEN = 'bbbbbbbb-bbbb-4bbb-8bbb-bbbbbbbbbbbb';

const usedParameters = new Map([
  [SPEED, { id: SPEED, name: 'Speed', minValue: 0, maxValue: 10 }],
  [OPEN, { id: OPEN, name: 'Open scale', minValue: null, maxValue: null }],
]);
const knownStudentIds = new Set([S1, S2]);

function check(entries, periodNumber = 3) {
  return findProblems({ entries, usedParameters, knownStudentIds, periodNumber });
}

test('accepts valid entries, including the range limits', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(
    check([
      { studentId: S1, parameterId: SPEED, value: 0 },
      { studentId: S1, parameterId: OPEN, value: 10 },
      { studentId: S2, parameterId: SPEED, value: 10 },
    ]),
    []
  );
});

test('rejects values outside a parameter range', () => {
  const problems = check([
    { studentId: S1, parameterId: SPEED, value: 11 },
    { studentId: S2, parameterId: SPEED, value: -0.5 },
  ]);
  assert.deepEqual(
    problems.map((p) => [p.index, p.message]),
    [
      [0, 'Speed must be at most 10.'],
      [1, 'Speed must be at least 0.'],
    ]
  );
});

test('rejects a parameter that the period does not use', () => {
  const problems = check(
    [{ studentId: S1, parameterId: 'cccccccc-cccc-4ccc-8ccc-cccccccccccc', value: 5 }],
    7
  );
  assert.deepEqual(
    problems.map((p) => p.message),
    ['This parameter is not used in Period 7.']
  );
});

test('rejects unknown students', () => {
  const problems = check([
    { studentId: '33333333-3333-4333-8333-333333333333', parameterId: SPEED, value: 5 },
  ]);
  assert.deepEqual(
    problems.map((p) => p.message),
    ['Student not found.']
  );
});

test('rejects the same student and parameter twice', () => {
  const problems = check([
    { studentId: S1, parameterId: SPEED, value: 5 },
    { studentId: S1, parameterId: SPEED, value: 6 },
  ]);
  assert.deepEqual(
    problems.map((p) => [p.index, p.message]),
    [[1, 'This student and parameter appear more than once.']]
  );
});

test('null clears a cell even when a range exists; open scales accept any number', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(
    check([
      { studentId: S1, parameterId: SPEED, value: null },
      { studentId: S1, parameterId: OPEN, value: 12345.5 },
    ]),
    []
  );
});

test('checks the shape of the request', () => {
  assert.equal(validateShape([{ studentId: S1, parameterId: SPEED, value: 5 }]), null);
  assert.equal(validateShape([{ studentId: S1, parameterId: SPEED, value: null }]), null);
  assert.notEqual(validateShape([]), null);
  assert.notEqual(validateShape('nope'), null);
  assert.notEqual(validateShape([{ studentId: 'x', parameterId: SPEED, value: 5 }]), null);
  assert.notEqual(validateShape([{ studentId: S1, parameterId: SPEED, value: '5' }]), null);
  assert.notEqual(validateShape([{ studentId: S1, parameterId: SPEED }]), null);
  assert.notEqual(validateShape([{ studentId: S1, parameterId: SPEED, value: 1e12 }]), null);
});
Run:

npm test
You should see 7 new green tests, for a total of pass 23 and fail 0 (9 + 7 + 7). If any fail, send me the output before continuing.

C. The routes

Create src/routes/evaluations.js:

js
import { Router } from 'express';
import pool from '../db/pool.js';
import { isUuid } from '../lib/ids.js';
import { periodForDate, todayIso } from '../lib/periods.js';
import {
  MAX_PERIOD,
  isValidPeriodNumber,
  parametersUsedInPeriod,
} from '../lib/parameterUse.js';
import { findProblems, validateShape } from '../lib/evaluationCheck.js';

const router = Router();

router.get('/evaluations', async (req, res) => {
  const { studentId, parameterId, periodNumber } = req.query;
  const conditions = ['value IS NOT NULL'];
  const values = [];

  if (studentId !== undefined) {
    if (!isUuid(studentId)) {
      return res.status(400).json({ error: 'studentId must be a valid ID.' });
    }
    values.push(studentId);
    conditions.push(`student_id = $${values.length}`);
  }
  if (parameterId !== undefined) {
    if (!isUuid(parameterId)) {
      return res.status(400).json({ error: 'parameterId must be a valid ID.' });
    }
    values.push(parameterId);
    conditions.push(`parameter_id = $${values.length}`);
  }
  if (periodNumber !== undefined) {
    const isDigits =
      typeof periodNumber === 'string' && /^\d+$/.test(periodNumber);
    if (!isDigits || !isValidPeriodNumber(Number(periodNumber))) {
      return res.status(400).json({
        error: `periodNumber must be a whole number from 1 to ${MAX_PERIOD}.`,
      });
    }
    values.push(Number(periodNumber));
    conditions.push(`period_number = $${values.length}`);
  }

  if (values.length === 0) {
    return res.status(400).json({
      error: 'Add at least one filter: studentId, parameterId or periodNumber.',
    });
  }

  const result = await pool.query(
    `SELECT student_id    AS "studentId",
            parameter_id  AS "parameterId",
            period_number AS "periodNumber",
            value::float8 AS value,
            updated_at    AS "updatedAt"
     FROM evaluations
     WHERE ${conditions.join(' AND ')}
     ORDER BY period_number, student_id, parameter_id
     LIMIT 20000`,
    values
  );
  res.json({ evaluations: result.rows });
});

router.put('/evaluations/bulk', async (req, res) => {
  const { periodNumber, entries } = req.body ?? {};

  if (!isValidPeriodNumber(periodNumber)) {
    return res.status(400).json({
      error: `periodNumber must be a whole number from 1 to ${MAX_PERIOD}.`,
    });
  }
  const shapeError = validateShape(entries);
  if (shapeError) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: shapeError });
  }

  const anchorResult = await pool.query(
    'SELECT value FROM settings WHERE key = $1',
    ['period_anchor_date']
  );
  const anchorDate = anchorResult.rows[0]?.value;
  if (!anchorDate) {
    return res.status(409).json({ error: 'Set the period anchor date first.' });
  }

  const current = periodForDate(anchorDate, todayIso());
  if (current === null) {
    return res.status(409).json({ error: 'Period 1 has not started yet.' });
  }
  if (periodNumber > current) {
    return res.status(400).json({
      error: `Period ${periodNumber} has not started yet. The current period is ${current}.`,
    });
  }

  const studentIds = [...new Set(entries.map((entry) => entry.studentId))];
  const [changesResult, parametersResult, studentsResult] = await Promise.all([
    pool.query(
      `SELECT parameter_id  AS "parameterId",
              period_number AS "periodNumber",
              scope,
              action
       FROM parameter_changes`
    ),
    pool.query(
      `SELECT id,
              name,
              min_value::float8 AS "minValue",
              max_value::float8 AS "maxValue"
       FROM parameters`
    ),
    pool.query(
      `SELECT id FROM students
       WHERE id = ANY($1::uuid[]) AND deleted_at IS NULL`,
      [studentIds]
    ),
  ]);

  const usedIds = new Set(
    parametersUsedInPeriod(changesResult.rows, periodNumber)
  );
  const usedParameters = new Map(
    parametersResult.rows
      .filter((parameter) => usedIds.has(parameter.id))
      .map((parameter) => [parameter.id, parameter])
  );
  const knownStudentIds = new Set(studentsResult.rows.map((row) => row.id));

  const problems = findProblems({
    entries,
    usedParameters,
    knownStudentIds,
    periodNumber,
  });
  if (problems.length > 0) {
    return res.status(400).json({
      error: 'Some entries are invalid. Nothing was saved.',
      problems,
    });
  }

  let changed = 0;
  const client = await pool.connect();
  try {
    await client.query('BEGIN');
    for (const entry of entries) {
      const result =
        entry.value === null
          ? await client.query(
              `UPDATE evaluations
               SET value = NULL, updated_at = now()
               WHERE student_id = $1 AND parameter_id = $2
                 AND period_number = $3 AND value IS NOT NULL`,
              [entry.studentId, entry.parameterId, periodNumber]
            )
          : await client.query(
              `INSERT INTO evaluations
                 (student_id, parameter_id, period_number, value)
               VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4)
               ON CONFLICT (student_id, parameter_id, period_number)
               DO UPDATE SET value = EXCLUDED.value, updated_at = now()
               WHERE evaluations.value IS DISTINCT FROM EXCLUDED.value`,
              [entry.studentId, entry.parameterId, periodNumber, entry.value]
            );
      changed += result.rowCount;
    }
    await client.query('COMMIT');
  } catch (err) {
    await client.query('ROLLBACK');
    throw err;
  } finally {
    client.release();
  }

  res.json({ periodNumber, changed });
});

export default router;
What the save route does, in order:

It checks the request's form. Any failure gives 400 with a clear message.
It requires an anchor date and works out the current period. Saving for a future period is refused.
It loads what it needs in one go: all the parameter changes, all parameters with their ranges, and the students named in the request (only active ones). Promise.all runs the three queries at the same time.
It runs our tested parametersUsedInPeriod to find which parameters this period uses. A score for any other parameter is refused.
It runs findProblems. If there's any problem, nothing is saved, and the answer lists each problem with its position.
Otherwise it saves everything inside one transaction. A score with a value is an upsert: insert, or update if it exists. A null empties an existing score and does nothing if there wasn't one.
IS DISTINCT FROM skips rows whose value didn't change, so updated_at isn't touched needlessly. That's why saving the same values twice reports changed: 0.
Connect it in src/index.js. Add the import with the others:

js
import evaluationsRouter from './routes/evaluations.js';
And this line after app.use('/api', parametersRouter);:

js
app.use('/api', evaluationsRouter);
The two locks. These edit code you already have.

In src/routes/periods.js, replace the whole router.put('/settings/period-anchor', ...) block with:

js
router.put('/settings/period-anchor', async (req, res) => {
  const anchorDate = req.body?.anchorDate;
  if (!isValidIsoDate(anchorDate)) {
    return res
      .status(400)
      .json({ error: 'anchorDate must be a real date written as YYYY-MM-DD.' });
  }
  if (!isSaturday(anchorDate)) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'The anchor date must be a Saturday.' });
  }

  if ((await readAnchor()) !== anchorDate) {
    const scores = await pool.query('SELECT 1 FROM evaluations LIMIT 1');
    if (scores.rowCount > 0) {
      return res.status(409).json({
        error: 'The anchor date cannot be changed after scores exist.',
      });
    }
  }

  await pool.query(
    `INSERT INTO settings (key, value)
     VALUES ($1, $2)
     ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE
       SET value = EXCLUDED.value, updated_at = now()`,
    [ANCHOR_KEY, anchorDate]
  );
  res.json({ anchorDate });
});
In src/routes/parameters.js, inside router.patch('/parameters/:id', ...), add this block right after the if (entries.length === 0) { ... } check and before const assignments:

js
  if ('minValue' in parsed.values || 'maxValue' in parsed.values) {
    const currentResult = await pool.query(
      `SELECT min_value::float8 AS "minValue", max_value::float8 AS "maxValue"
       FROM parameters WHERE id = $1`,
      [req.params.id]
    );
    if (currentResult.rowCount === 0) {
      return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Parameter not found.' });
    }
    const row = currentResult.rows[0];
    const rangeChanges =
      ('minValue' in parsed.values && parsed.values.minValue !== row.minValue) ||
      ('maxValue' in parsed.values && parsed.values.maxValue !== row.maxValue);

    if (rangeChanges) {
      const scores = await pool.query(
        'SELECT 1 FROM evaluations WHERE parameter_id = $1 LIMIT 1',
        [req.params.id]
      );
      if (scores.rowCount > 0) {
        return res.status(409).json({
          error: 'The range cannot be changed after scores exist for this parameter.',
        });
      }
    }
  }
Sending the same range values again is allowed. Only a real change is blocked.

Piece 14: navigation and the Settings page
Currently the app shows one page. With several, the address bar has to match the screen (/students, /settings, ...) so that links, refresh, and the back button all work. That's what a router does. Current React Router (version 7) is used through the single react-router package, with BrowserRouter, Routes, Route, and NavLink for basic client-side routing.

A. Install

B. Turn on the router

Replace all of src/main.jsx with:

jsx
import { StrictMode } from 'react';
import { createRoot } from 'react-dom/client';
import { BrowserRouter } from 'react-router';
import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import './index.css';
import App from './App.jsx';

const queryClient = new QueryClient({
  defaultOptions: { queries: { retry: 1 } },
});

createRoot(document.getElementById('root')).render(
  <StrictMode>
    <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
      <BrowserRouter>
        <App />
      </BrowserRouter>
    </QueryClientProvider>
  </StrictMode>
);
BrowserRouter works like QueryClientProvider: it makes the router's information (what the address is, how to navigate) available to every component inside it.

C. Small helpers

Replace all of src/lib/format.js with:

js
export function formatStudentCode(code) {
  return `${code.slice(0, 4)}-${code.slice(4)}`;
}

export function formatDateTime(isoString) {
  return new Date(isoString).toLocaleString();
}

export function formatIsoDate(isoDate) {
  const [year, month, day] = isoDate.split('-').map(Number);
  return new Date(year, month - 1, day).toLocaleDateString(undefined, {
    weekday: 'short',
    day: 'numeric',
    month: 'short',
    year: 'numeric',
  });
}

export function toIsoDate(date) {
  const year = date.getFullYear();
  const month = String(date.getMonth() + 1).padStart(2, '0');
  const day = String(date.getDate()).padStart(2, '0');
  return `${year}-${month}-${day}`;
}
The first two functions are unchanged. The new ones work with plain dates like 2026-09-19. formatIsoDate builds the date from its parts (year, month, day) on purpose. Writing new Date('2026-09-19') would treat it as midnight in UTC, and in time zones behind UTC it would display as the previous day.

Create src/api/periods.js:

js
import { request } from './http.js';

export function getPeriods() {
  return request('/api/periods');
}

export function setPeriodAnchor(anchorDate) {
  return request('/api/settings/period-anchor', {
    method: 'PUT',
    body: JSON.stringify({ anchorDate }),
  });
}
Create src/lib/usePeriods.js:

js
import { useQuery } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { getPeriods } from '../api/periods.js';

export function usePeriods() {
  return useQuery({ queryKey: ['periods'], queryFn: getPeriods });
}
This is a custom hook again. Several pages will need the period information (Settings now, Evaluate later), so it lives in one place. Any page that calls usePeriods() shares the same cached data.

D. The layout, the pages, and the routes

Create src/components/Layout.jsx:

jsx
import { NavLink, Outlet } from 'react-router';

function Layout() {
  return (
    <>
      <header className="topbar">
        <nav>
          <NavLink to="/students">Students</NavLink>
          <NavLink to="/settings">Settings</NavLink>
        </nav>
      </header>
      <Outlet />
    </>
  );

export default Layout;
NavLink is a link that changes the address without reloading the page. It also gives the link a class called active when its address is the current one, which we style in a moment.
Outlet is the "hole" where the page for the current address is drawn. The header stays put, and only the page below it changes.

Create src/pages/SettingsPage.jsx:

jsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import { useMutation, useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { setPeriodAnchor } from '../api/periods.js';
import { formatIsoDate, toIsoDate } from '../lib/format.js';
import { usePeriods } from '../lib/usePeriods.js';

function lastSaturday() {
  const date = new Date();
  date.setDate(date.getDate() - ((date.getDay() + 1) % 7));
  return toIsoDate(date);
}

function isSaturday(isoDate) {
  const [year, month, day] = isoDate.split('-').map(Number);
  return new Date(year, month - 1, day).getDay() === 6;
}

function PeriodSummary({ data }) {
  if (!data.anchorDate) {
    return <p>No start date is set yet. Pick one below to begin.</p>;
  }
  if (data.current === null) {
    return <p>Period 1 starts on {formatIsoDate(data.anchorDate)}.</p>;
  }
  const currentPeriod = data.periods[data.periods.length - 1];
  return (
    <p>
      Today is {formatIsoDate(data.today)}. The current period is{' '}
      <strong>Period {data.current}</strong> (
      {formatIsoDate(currentPeriod.startDate)} to{' '}
      {formatIsoDate(currentPeriod.endDate)}).
    </p>
  );
}

function AnchorForm({ savedAnchor }) {
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
  const [anchorDate, setAnchorDate] = useState(savedAnchor ?? lastSaturday());
  const [problem, setProblem] = useState(null);

  const mutation = useMutation({
    mutationFn: setPeriodAnchor,
    onSuccess: () => queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['periods'] }),
  });

  function handleSubmit(event) {
    event.preventDefault();
    if (!anchorDate || !isSaturday(anchorDate)) {
      mutation.reset();
      setProblem('Please pick a Saturday.');
      return;
    }
    setProblem(null);
    mutation.mutate(anchorDate);
  }

  return (
    <form onSubmit={handleSubmit}>
      <label htmlFor="anchorDate">First day of Period 1 (a Saturday)</label>
      <div className="row">
        <input
          id="anchorDate"
          type="date"
          value={anchorDate}
          onChange={(event) => setAnchorDate(event.target.value)}
        />
        <button type="submit" disabled={mutation.isPending}>
          {mutation.isPending ? 'Saving...' : 'Save'}
        </button>
      </div>
      {problem && <p className="error">{problem}</p>}
      {mutation.isError && <p className="error">{mutation.error.message}</p>}
      {mutation.isSuccess && <p className="success">Saved.</p>}
    </form>
  );
}

function SettingsPage() {
  const { data, isPending, isError, error } = usePeriods();

  return (
    <main>
      <h1>Settings</h1>
      <section>
        <h2>Periods</h2>
        <p>
          Evaluations are grouped into periods. Each period is one week, from
          Saturday to Friday, numbered from Period 1. Period 1 starts on the
          date below.
        </p>
        {isPending && <p>Loading...</p>}
        {isError && <p className="error">{error.message}</p>}
        {data && <PeriodSummary data={data} />}
        {data && <AnchorForm savedAnchor={data.anchorDate} />}
        <p className="hint">
          The start date can only be changed until the first score is saved,
          because changing it would move every saved score to a different week.
        </p>
      </section>
    </main>
  );
}

export default SettingsPage;
<input type="date"> gives a date picker, and its value is text like 2026-09-19, the same format the server uses.
lastSaturday() suggests the most recent Saturday as the starting value. getDay() counts Sunday as 0 through Saturday as 6, so (day + 1) % 7 is "days since Saturday."
The check for a Saturday runs in the browser for instant feedback. The server checks again and also enforces the lock, and when it refuses, its message shows in red.
There are three small components in one file: PeriodSummary, AnchorForm, and SettingsPage. That's fine for pieces that belong together, and each has its own job.
Replace all of src/App.jsx with:

jsx
import { Navigate, Route, Routes } from 'react-router';
import Layout from './components/Layout.jsx';
import NotFoundPage from './pages/NotFoundPage.jsx';
import SettingsPage from './pages/SettingsPage.jsx';
import StudentsPage from './pages/StudentsPage.jsx';

function App() {
  return (
    <Routes>
      <Route element={<Layout />}>
        <Route path="/" element={<Navigate to="/students" replace />} />
        <Route path="/students" element={<StudentsPage />} />
        <Route path="/settings" element={<SettingsPage />} />
        <Route path="*" element={<NotFoundPage />} />
      </Route>
    </Routes>
  );
}

export default App;
Routes looks at the address and shows the first Route whose path matches. element is what to draw.
A Route with no path but with element={<Layout />} wraps everything inside it. Its Outlet shows whichever child route matched.
Navigate sends / to /students. replace swaps the history entry, so the back button doesn't bounce.
path="*" matches everything else, which is our "not found" page.
Add this to the end of src/index.css:

css

}

.topbar nav {

  gap: 1rem;
}

.topbar a {
  color: inherit;
  text-decoration: none;
  padding-bottom: 0.25rem;
}

.topbar a.active {
  font-weight: 600;
  border-bottom: 2px solid currentColor;
}

.hint {
  font-size: 0.9rem;
  opacity: 0.7;
}
