cd /data/projects/student-panel/client
A. API functions and helpers

Create src/api/parameters.js:

js
import { request } from './http.js';

export async function listParameters() {
  const data = await request('/api/parameters');
  return data.parameters;
}

export function createParameter(input) {
  return request('/api/parameters', {
    method: 'POST',
    body: JSON.stringify(input),
  });
}

export function updateParameter(id, fields) {
  return request(`/api/parameters/${id}`, {
    method: 'PATCH',
    body: JSON.stringify(fields),
  });
}

export async function listParameterChanges() {
  const data = await request('/api/parameter-changes');
  return data.changes;
}

export function deleteParameterChange(id) {
  return request(`/api/parameter-changes/${id}`, { method: 'DELETE' });
}
Create src/lib/numbers.js:

js
export function parseOptionalNumber(text) {
  const trimmed = text.trim();
  if (trimmed === '') return { value: null };
  const number = Number(trimmed);
  return Number.isFinite(number) ? { value: number } : { error: true };
}

export function numberToText(value) {
  return value === null ? '' : String(value);
}
Number boxes hold their content as text in React, and we only convert to a number when the form is submitted. That's because while someone is typing, the box may briefly hold things like - or 1., which aren't valid numbers yet. An empty box means "no limit," which becomes null.

Add these two functions to the end of src/lib/format.js:

js
export function describeChange({ scope, action }) {
  const verb = action === 'add' ? 'Added' : 'Removed';
  return scope === 'from_here_on'
    ? `${verb}, from this period on`
    : `${verb}, this period only`;
}

export function formatRange({ minValue, maxValue }) {
  if (minValue === null && maxValue === null) return '—';
  return `${minValue ?? '…'} to ${maxValue ?? '…'}`;
}
B. The "add a parameter" form

Create src/components/ParameterForm.jsx:

jsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import { useMutation, useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { createParameter } from '../api/parameters.js';
import { parseOptionalNumber } from '../lib/numbers.js';
import { usePeriods } from '../lib/usePeriods.js';

function ParameterForm() {
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
  const { data: periods } = usePeriods();

  const [name, setName] = useState('');
  const [unit, setUnit] = useState('');
  const [minText, setMinText] = useState('');
  const [maxText, setMaxText] = useState('');
  const [startText, setStartText] = useState(null);
  const [problem, setProblem] = useState(null);

  const shownStart = startText ?? String(periods?.current ?? 1);

  const mutation = useMutation({
    mutationFn: createParameter,
    onSuccess: () => {
      setName('');
      setUnit('');
      setMinText('');
      setMaxText('');
      setStartText(null);
      queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['parameters'] });
      queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['parameter-changes'] });
    },
  });

  function fail(message) {
    mutation.reset();
    setProblem(message);
  }

  function handleSubmit(event) {
    event.preventDefault();

    const min = parseOptionalNumber(minText);
    const max = parseOptionalNumber(maxText);
    const startPeriod = Number(shownStart);

    if (name.trim() === '') return fail('Please enter a name.');
    if (min.error || max.error) {
      return fail('Minimum and maximum must be numbers, or left empty.');
    }
    if (min.value !== null && max.value !== null && min.value > max.value) {
      return fail('The minimum cannot be larger than the maximum.');
    }
    if (!Number.isInteger(startPeriod) || startPeriod < 1) {
      return fail('The starting period must be a whole number from 1.');
    }

    setProblem(null);
    mutation.mutate({
      name,
      unit: unit.trim() === '' ? null : unit,
      minValue: min.value,
      maxValue: max.value,
      startPeriod,
    });
  }

  return (
    <form onSubmit={handleSubmit}>
      <div className="form-grid">
        <label>
          Name
          <input
            value={name}
            onChange={(event) => setName(event.target.value)}
            placeholder="e.g. Speed"
            maxLength={60}
            autoComplete="off"
          />
        </label>
        <label>
          Unit (optional)
          <input
            value={unit}
            onChange={(event) => setUnit(event.target.value)}
            placeholder="e.g. km/h"
            maxLength={20}
            autoComplete="off"
          />
        </label>
        <label>
          Minimum (optional)
          <input
            type="number"
            step="any"
            value={minText}
            onChange={(event) => setMinText(event.target.value)}
          />
        </label>
        <label>
          Maximum (optional)
          <input
            type="number"
            step="any"
            value={maxText}
            onChange={(event) => setMaxText(event.target.value)}
          />
        </label>
        <label>
          Used from period
          <input
            type="number"
            min="1"
            step="1"
            value={shownStart}
            onChange={(event) => setStartText(event.target.value)}
          />
        </label>
      </div>

      <button type="submit" disabled={mutation.isPending}>
        {mutation.isPending ? 'Adding...' : 'Add parameter'}
      </button>

      {problem && <p className="error">{problem}</p>}
      {mutation.isError && <p className="error">{mutation.error.message}</p>}
      {mutation.isSuccess && (
        <p className="success">
          Added {mutation.data.name}, used from Period{' '}
          {mutation.variables.startPeriod} on.
        </p>
      )}
    </form>
  );
}

export default ParameterForm;
"Used from period" starts out showing the current period. startText is null until you type, and startText ?? ... shows the default until then.
Adding a parameter creates two things on the server: the parameter and its first "added from Period N on" change. That's why success refreshes both the parameters and parameter-changes lists.
The browser checks what it can (empty name, min above max). The server has the final say on things only it knows, like a duplicate name, and its message shows in red.
mutation.variables holds what we sent, so the success message can repeat the starting period.
C. The parameter list with inline editing

Create src/components/ParameterRow.jsx:

jsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import { useMutation, useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { updateParameter } from '../api/parameters.js';
import { formatRange } from '../lib/format.js';
import { numberToText, parseOptionalNumber } from '../lib/numbers.js';

function ParameterRow({ parameter }) {
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
  const [isEditing, setIsEditing] = useState(false);
  const [name, setName] = useState(parameter.name);
  const [unit, setUnit] = useState(parameter.unit ?? '');
  const [minText, setMinText] = useState(numberToText(parameter.minValue));
  const [maxText, setMaxText] = useState(numberToText(parameter.maxValue));
  const [problem, setProblem] = useState(null);

  const update = useMutation({
    mutationFn: (fields) => updateParameter(parameter.id, fields),
    onSuccess: () => {
      setIsEditing(false);
      queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['parameters'] });
    },
  });

  function startEditing() {
    setName(parameter.name);
    setUnit(parameter.unit ?? '');
    setMinText(numberToText(parameter.minValue));
    setMaxText(numberToText(parameter.maxValue));
    setProblem(null);
    update.reset();
    setIsEditing(true);
  }

  function handleSave(event) {
    event.preventDefault();

    const min = parseOptionalNumber(minText);
    const max = parseOptionalNumber(maxText);

    if (name.trim() === '') {
      setProblem('Please enter a name.');
      return;
    }
    if (min.error || max.error) {
      setProblem('Minimum and maximum must be numbers, or left empty.');
      return;
    }
    if (min.value !== null && max.value !== null && min.value > max.value) {
      setProblem('The minimum cannot be larger than the maximum.');
      return;
    }

    setProblem(null);
    update.mutate({
      name,
      unit: unit.trim() === '' ? null : unit,
      minValue: min.value,
      maxValue: max.value,
    });
  }

  if (isEditing) {
    return (
      <tr>
        <td colSpan={4}>
          <form onSubmit={handleSave} className="edit-form">
            <label>
              Name
              <input
                value={name}
                onChange={(event) => setName(event.target.value)}
                maxLength={60}
                autoComplete="off"
              />
            </label>
            <label>
              Unit (optional)
              <input
                value={unit}
                onChange={(event) => setUnit(event.target.value)}
                maxLength={20}
                autoComplete="off"
              />
            </label>
            <label>
              Minimum (optional)
              <input
                type="number"
                step="any"
                value={minText}
                onChange={(event) => setMinText(event.target.value)}
              />
            </label>
            <label>
              Maximum (optional)
              <input
                type="number"
                step="any"
                value={maxText}
                onChange={(event) => setMaxText(event.target.value)}
              />
            </label>
            <div className="row">
              <button type="submit" disabled={update.isPending}>
                Save
              </button>
              <button type="button" onClick={() => setIsEditing(false)}>
                Cancel
              </button>
            </div>
            {problem && <p className="error">{problem}</p>}
            {update.isError && <p className="error">{update.error.message}</p>}
          </form>
        </td>
      </tr>
    );
  }

  return (
    <tr>
      <td>{parameter.name}</td>
      <td>{parameter.unit ?? '—'}</td>
      <td>{formatRange(parameter)}</td>
      <td className="actions">
        <button type="button" onClick={startEditing}>
          Edit
        </button>
      </td>
    </tr>
  );
}

export default ParameterRow;
It follows the same pattern as StudentRow: each row keeps its own "am I being edited?" memory. If the server refuses a change (for example, the range of a parameter that already has scores), its message appears under the form.

Create src/components/ParametersTable.jsx:

jsx
import { useQuery } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { listParameters } from '../api/parameters.js';
import ParameterRow from './ParameterRow.jsx';

function ParametersTable() {
  const {
    data: parameters,
    isPending,
    isError,
    error,
  } = useQuery({ queryKey: ['parameters'], queryFn: listParameters });

  if (isPending) return <p>Loading parameters...</p>;
  if (isError) return <p className="error">{error.message}</p>;
  if (parameters.length === 0) {
    return <p>No parameters yet. Add the first one above.</p>;
  }

  return (
    <div className="table-wrap">
      <table>
        <thead>
          <tr>
            <th>Name</th>
            <th>Unit</th>
            <th>Range</th>
            <th>Actions</th>
          </tr>
        </thead>
        <tbody>
          {parameters.map((parameter) => (
            <ParameterRow key={parameter.id} parameter={parameter} />
          ))}
        </tbody>
      </table>
    </div>
  );
}

export default ParametersTable;
D. The change history with Undo

Create src/components/ChangeHistory.jsx:

jsx
import { useMutation, useQuery, useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { deleteParameterChange, listParameterChanges } from '../api/parameters.js';
import { describeChange, formatDateTime } from '../lib/format.js';

function ChangeRow({ change }) {
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();

  const undo = useMutation({
    mutationFn: () => deleteParameterChange(change.id),
    onSuccess: () => {
      queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['parameter-changes'] });
      queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['period-parameters'] });
    },
  });

  function handleUndo() {
    const ok = window.confirm(
      `Undo this change to ${change.parameterName} (Period ${change.periodNumber})? ` +
        'Periods will use parameters as if it never happened. ' +
        'Saved scores are never deleted.'
    );
    if (ok) undo.mutate();
  }

  return (
    <tr>
      <td>Period {change.periodNumber}</td>
      <td>{change.parameterName}</td>
      <td>{describeChange(change)}</td>
      <td>{formatDateTime(change.createdAt)}</td>
      <td className="actions">
        <button type="button" onClick={handleUndo} disabled={undo.isPending}>
          {undo.isPending ? 'Undoing...' : 'Undo'}
        </button>
        {undo.isError && <p className="error">{undo.error.message}</p>}
      </td>
    </tr>
  );
}

function ChangeHistory() {
  const {
    data: changes,
    isPending,
    isError,
    error,
  } = useQuery({
    queryKey: ['parameter-changes'],
    queryFn: listParameterChanges,
  });

  if (isPending) return <p>Loading history...</p>;
  if (isError) return <p className="error">{error.message}</p>;
  if (changes.length === 0) return <p>No changes yet.</p>;

  return (
    <div className="table-wrap">
      <table>
        <thead>
          <tr>
            <th>From</th>
            <th>Parameter</th>
            <th>Change</th>
            <th>Recorded</th>
            <th>Actions</th>
          </tr>
        </thead>
        <tbody>
          {changes.map((change) => (
            <ChangeRow key={change.id} change={change} />
          ))}
        </tbody>
      </table>
    </div>
  );
}

export default ChangeHistory;
Undo deletes one row from the log, and the rule from the design recalculates everything. Nothing else needs fixing up. The period-parameters refresh is there for a screen we'll add soon. It does nothing harmful until then.

Piece 16: the Evaluate page

cd /data/projects/student-panel/client
A. Keep server details in errors

When the server refuses a save, it lists which cells are wrong. Our request helper currently throws that detail away. Replace all of src/api/http.js with:

js
export async function request(url, options = {}) {
  let response;
  try {
    response = await fetch(url, {
      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
      ...options,
    });
  } catch {
    throw new Error('Cannot reach the server. Is it running?');
  }

  let data = null;
  try {
    data = await response.json();
  } catch {
    // the answer had no JSON body
  }

  if (!response.ok) {
    const error = new Error(
      data?.error ??
        `Request failed (status ${response.status}). If this keeps happening, check that the server is running.`
    );
    error.status = response.status;
    error.data = data;
    throw error;
  }
  return data;
}
The only change is that the error now carries status and data, so the screen can read the server's problems list.

B. API functions

Create src/api/evaluations.js:

js
import { request } from './http.js';

export async function listPeriodParameters(periodNumber) {
  const data = await request(`/api/periods/${periodNumber}/parameters`);
  return data.parameters;
}

export async function listEvaluations({ periodNumber }) {
  const data = await request(`/api/evaluations?periodNumber=${periodNumber}`);
  return data.evaluations;
}

export function saveEvaluations({ periodNumber, entries }) {
  return request('/api/evaluations/bulk', {
    method: 'PUT',
    body: JSON.stringify({ periodNumber, entries }),
  });
}
C. The cell logic, with tests

Reading what someone typed into a cell is the risky part, so it goes into plain functions we can test. Create src/lib/cells.js:

js
const NUMBER_PATTERN = /^[+-]?(\d+([.,]\d*)?|[.,]\d+)$/;

export function cellKey(studentId, parameterId) {
  return `${studentId}:${parameterId}`;
}

export function savedText(value) {
  return value === null || value === undefined ? '' : String(value);
}

export function parseCell(text, parameter) {
  const trimmed = text.trim();
  if (trimmed === '') return { value: null };
  if (!NUMBER_PATTERN.test(trimmed)) return { error: 'Enter a number.' };

  const value = Number(trimmed.replace(',', '.'));
  if (parameter.minValue !== null && value < parameter.minValue) {
    return { error: `At least ${parameter.minValue}.` };
  }
  if (parameter.maxValue !== null && value > parameter.maxValue) {
    return { error: `At most ${parameter.maxValue}.` };
  }
  return { value };
}

export function buildEntries(drafts, parametersById) {
  const entries = [];
  const errors = {};

  for (const [key, text] of Object.entries(drafts)) {
    const [studentId, parameterId] = key.split(':');
    const parameter = parametersById.get(parameterId);
    if (!parameter) continue;

    const result = parseCell(text, parameter);
    if (result.error) {
      errors[key] = result.error;
    } else {
      entries.push({ studentId, parameterId, value: result.value });
    }
  }
  return { entries, errors };
}
cellKey names one cell: student plus parameter. Every cell of the grid is found by that key.
parseCell turns typed text into a number. Empty means "clear this cell" (null). The pattern accepts only plain numbers, so things like 1e3 or 0x10, which JavaScript would otherwise happily convert, are rejected. A comma counts as a decimal point.
buildEntries turns all edited cells into the list the server expects, and collects an error message per bad cell.
Create test/cells.test.js:

js
import { test } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { buildEntries, cellKey, parseCell, savedText } from '../src/lib/cells.js';

const speed = { minValue: 0, maxValue: 10 };
const open = { minValue: null, maxValue: null };

test('empty text clears the cell', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(parseCell('', speed), { value: null });
  assert.deepEqual(parseCell('   ', speed), { value: null });
});

test('accepts plain numbers, including a decimal comma', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(parseCell('7', speed), { value: 7 });
  assert.deepEqual(parseCell('6.5', speed), { value: 6.5 });
  assert.deepEqual(parseCell('6,5', speed), { value: 6.5 });
  assert.deepEqual(parseCell(' 0 ', speed), { value: 0 });
  assert.deepEqual(parseCell('-3', open), { value: -3 });
});

test('rejects things that are not plain numbers', () => {
  for (const text of ['abc', '1e3', '0x10', '5 5', '--1', '.', '7%']) {
    assert.ok(parseCell(text, open).error, `${text} should be rejected`);
  }
});

test('enforces the range', () => {
  assert.equal(parseCell('11', speed).error, 'At most 10.');
  assert.equal(parseCell('-1', speed).error, 'At least 0.');
  assert.deepEqual(parseCell('10', speed), { value: 10 });
});

test('builds entries and collects errors per cell', () => {
  const parameters = new Map([['p1', speed]]);
  const drafts = {
    [cellKey('s1', 'p1')]: '8',
    [cellKey('s2', 'p1')]: '15',
    [cellKey('s3', 'p1')]: '',
  };
  const { entries, errors } = buildEntries(drafts, parameters);
  assert.deepEqual(entries, [
    { studentId: 's1', parameterId: 'p1', value: 8 },
    { studentId: 's3', parameterId: 'p1', value: null },
  ]);
  assert.deepEqual(errors, { 's2:p1': 'At most 10.' });
});

test('saved values are shown as plain text', () => {
  assert.equal(savedText(7), '7');
  assert.equal(savedText(6.5), '6.5');
  assert.equal(savedText(undefined), '');
  assert.equal(savedText(null), '');
});
Run:

npm pkg set scripts.test="node --test"
npm test
You should see pass 6 and fail 0. If anything fails, send me the output before going on.

D. The period picker

Create src/components/PeriodPicker.jsx:

jsx
import { formatIsoDate } from '../lib/format.js';

function PeriodPicker({ periods, value, onChange }) {
  const list = periods.periods;
  const selected = list.find((period) => period.number === value);

  return (
    <div className="period-picker">
      <button type="button" onClick={() => onChange(value - 1)} disabled={value <= 1}>
        Previous
      </button>
      <select
        aria-label="Period"
        value={value}
        onChange={(event) => onChange(Number(event.target.value))}
      >
        {[...list].reverse().map((period) => (
          <option key={period.number} value={period.number}>
            Period {period.number}
            {period.number === periods.current ? ' (current)' : ''}
          </option>
        ))}
      </select>
      <button
        type="button"
        onClick={() => onChange(value + 1)}
        disabled={value >= periods.current}
      >
        Next
      </button>
      {selected && (
        <span className="hint">
          {formatIsoDate(selected.startDate)} to {formatIsoDate(selected.endDate)}
        </span>
      )}
    </div>
  );
}

export default PeriodPicker;
It only offers periods from 1 up to the current one, newest first, because scores can't be entered for future periods. It doesn't decide anything itself. It reports the chosen number through onChange, and the page decides what to do.

E. The grid

Create src/components/EvaluationGrid.jsx:

jsx
import { useEffect } from 'react';
import { keepPreviousData, useQuery } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { listStudents } from '../api/students.js';
import { cellKey, savedText } from '../lib/cells.js';
import { formatRange, formatStudentCode } from '../lib/format.js';

const GRID_PAGE_SIZE = 50;

function rangeHint(parameter) {
  const range = formatRange(parameter);
  return [range === '—' ? null : range, parameter.unit]
    .filter(Boolean)
    .join(' · ');
}

function EvaluationGrid({
  parameters,
  saved,
  drafts,
  cellErrors,
  onCellChange,
  search,
  page,
  onPageChange,
}) {
  const { data, isPending, isError, error, isPlaceholderData } = useQuery({
    queryKey: ['students', { search, page, pageSize: GRID_PAGE_SIZE }],
    queryFn: () => listStudents({ search, page, pageSize: GRID_PAGE_SIZE }),
    placeholderData: keepPreviousData,
  });

  const totalPages = data
    ? Math.max(1, Math.ceil(data.total / data.pageSize))
    : 1;

  useEffect(() => {
    if (data && page > totalPages) onPageChange(totalPages);
  }, [data, page, totalPages, onPageChange]);

  if (isPending) return <p>Loading students...</p>;
  if (isError) return <p className="error">{error.message}</p>;
  if (data.total === 0) {
    return (
      <p>
        {search
          ? `No students match "${search}".`
          : 'No students yet. Add some on the Students page.'}
      </p>
    );
  }
  if (parameters.length === 0) {
    return (
      <p>
        No parameters are used in this period. Check the change history on the
        Parameters page.
      </p>
    );
  }

  return (
    <>
      <div className="table-wrap">
        <table className={`grid${isPlaceholderData ? ' stale' : ''}`}>
          <thead>
            <tr>
              <th>Student</th>
              {parameters.map((parameter) => (
                <th key={parameter.id}>
                  {parameter.name}
                  <div className="hint">{rangeHint(parameter)}</div>
                </th>
              ))}
            </tr>
          </thead>
          <tbody>
            {data.students.map((student) => (
              <tr key={student.id}>
                <td>
                  {student.fullName}
                  <div className="hint code">
                    {formatStudentCode(student.studentCode)}
                  </div>
                </td>
                {parameters.map((parameter) => {
                  const key = cellKey(student.id, parameter.id);
                  const original = savedText(saved[key]);
                  const shown = drafts[key] ?? original;
                  const message = cellErrors[key];
                  const classes = [
                    'cell',
                    key in drafts ? 'changed' : '',
                    message ? 'invalid' : '',
                  ]
                    .filter(Boolean)
                    .join(' ');

                  return (
                    <td key={parameter.id}>
                      <input
                        className={classes}
                        inputMode="decimal"
                        value={shown}
                        onChange={(event) =>
                          onCellChange(key, event.target.value, original)
                        }
                        aria-label={`${parameter.name} for ${student.fullName}`}
                        autoComplete="off"
                      />
                      {message && <div className="error small">{message}</div>}
                    </td>
                  );
                })}
              </tr>
            ))}
          </tbody>
        </table>
      </div>

      <div className="pager">
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={() => onPageChange(page - 1)}
          disabled={page <= 1}
        >
          Previous
        </button>
        <span>
          Page {page} of {totalPages} ({data.total} students)
        </span>
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={() => onPageChange(page + 1)}
          disabled={page >= totalPages}
        >
          Next
        </button>
      </div>
    </>
  );
}

export default EvaluationGrid;
The grid holds no edits itself. It shows drafts[key] ?? original: what you've typed if you've changed that cell, otherwise the saved value. Changed cells get a changed class (yellow), and cells with a problem get invalid (red). inputMode="decimal" makes phones open a number keypad.

F. The page

Create src/pages/EvaluatePage.jsx:

jsx
import { useEffect, useMemo, useState } from 'react';
import { useMutation, useQuery, useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { Link } from 'react-router';
import {
  listEvaluations,
  listPeriodParameters,
  saveEvaluations,
} from '../api/evaluations.js';
import EvaluationGrid from '../components/EvaluationGrid.jsx';
import PeriodPicker from '../components/PeriodPicker.jsx';
import { buildEntries, cellKey } from '../lib/cells.js';
import { useDebouncedValue } from '../lib/useDebouncedValue.js';
import { usePeriods } from '../lib/usePeriods.js';

function EvaluatePage() {
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
  const periodsQuery = usePeriods();
  const periods = periodsQuery.data;

  const [chosenPeriod, setChosenPeriod] = useState(null);
  const [drafts, setDrafts] = useState({});
  const [searchText, setSearchText] = useState('');
  const [page, setPage] = useState(1);
  const search = useDebouncedValue(searchText.trim(), 300);

  const periodNumber = chosenPeriod ?? periods?.current ?? null;

  const parametersQuery = useQuery({
    queryKey: ['period-parameters', periodNumber],
    queryFn: () => listPeriodParameters(periodNumber),
    enabled: periodNumber !== null,
  });
  const savedQuery = useQuery({
    queryKey: ['evaluations', { periodNumber }],
    queryFn: () => listEvaluations({ periodNumber }),
    enabled: periodNumber !== null,
  });

  const saved = useMemo(
    () =>
      Object.fromEntries(
        (savedQuery.data ?? []).map((item) => [
          cellKey(item.studentId, item.parameterId),
          item.value,
        ])
      ),
    [savedQuery.data]
  );
  const parametersById = useMemo(
    () => new Map((parametersQuery.data ?? []).map((p) => [p.id, p])),
    [parametersQuery.data]
  );
  const liveErrors = useMemo(
    () => buildEntries(drafts, parametersById).errors,
    [drafts, parametersById]
  );

  const mutation = useMutation({
    mutationFn: saveEvaluations,
    onSuccess: async () => {
      await queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['evaluations'] });
      setDrafts({});
    },
  });

  const draftCount = Object.keys(drafts).length;
  const hasLiveErrors = Object.keys(liveErrors).length > 0;

  const serverErrors = Object.fromEntries(
    (mutation.error?.data?.problems ?? []).map((problem) => [
      cellKey(problem.studentId, problem.parameterId),
      problem.message,
    ])
  );
  const cellErrors = { ...serverErrors, ...liveErrors };

  useEffect(() => {
    if (draftCount === 0) return;
    function warn(event) {
      event.preventDefault();
    }
    window.addEventListener('beforeunload', warn);
    return () => window.removeEventListener('beforeunload', warn);
  }, [draftCount]);

  function handleCellChange(key, text, original) {
    if (!mutation.isIdle) mutation.reset();
    setDrafts((current) => {
      const next = { ...current };
      if (text.trim() === original) {
        delete next[key];
      } else {
        next[key] = text;
      }
      return next;
    });
  }

  function handlePeriodChange(next) {
    if (
      draftCount > 0 &&
      !window.confirm('You have unsaved changes. Discard them and switch period?')
    ) {
      return;
    }
    setChosenPeriod(next);
    setDrafts({});
    mutation.reset();
  }

  function handleSave() {
    const { entries, errors } = buildEntries(drafts, parametersById);
    if (Object.keys(errors).length > 0 || entries.length === 0) return;
    mutation.mutate({ periodNumber, entries });
  }

  function handleDiscard() {
    setDrafts({});
    mutation.reset();
  }

  function handleSearchChange(event) {
    setSearchText(event.target.value);
    setPage(1);
  }

  if (periodsQuery.isPending) {
    return (
      <main>
        <p>Loading...</p>
      </main>
    );
  }
  if (periodsQuery.isError) {
    return (
      <main>
        <p className="error">{periodsQuery.error.message}</p>
      </main>
    );
  }
  if (!periods.anchorDate) {
    return (
      <main>
        <h1>Evaluate</h1>
        <p>
          Set the period start date first, on the{' '}
          <Link to="/settings">Settings</Link> page.
        </p>
      </main>
    );
  }
  if (periods.current === null) {
    return (
      <main>
        <h1>Evaluate</h1>
        <p>Period 1 has not started yet.</p>
      </main>
    );
  }

  const isLoading = parametersQuery.isPending || savedQuery.isPending;
  const loadError = parametersQuery.error ?? savedQuery.error;

  let saveLabel = 'Save';
  if (mutation.isPending) {
    saveLabel = 'Saving...';
  } else if (draftCount > 0) {
    saveLabel = `Save ${draftCount} change${draftCount === 1 ? '' : 's'}`;
  }

  return (
    <main className="wide">
      <h1>Evaluate</h1>

      <PeriodPicker
        periods={periods}
        value={periodNumber}
        onChange={handlePeriodChange}
      />

      <div className="toolbar">
        <input
          type="search"
          className="search"
          value={searchText}
          onChange={handleSearchChange}
          placeholder="Search students by name or code"
          aria-label="Search students"
        />
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={handleSave}
          disabled={draftCount === 0 || hasLiveErrors || mutation.isPending}
        >
          {saveLabel}
        </button>
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={handleDiscard}
          disabled={draftCount === 0 || mutation.isPending}
        >
          Discard
        </button>
      </div>

      {hasLiveErrors && (
        <p className="error">Fix the red cells before saving.</p>
      )}
      {mutation.isError && <p className="error">{mutation.error.message}</p>}
      {mutation.isSuccess && (
        <p className="success">
          Saved. {mutation.data.changed} value
          {mutation.data.changed === 1 ? '' : 's'} changed.
        </p>
      )}

      {loadError && <p className="error">{loadError.message}</p>}
      {isLoading && !loadError && <p>Loading...</p>}
      {!isLoading && !loadError && (
        <EvaluationGrid
          parameters={parametersQuery.data}
          saved={saved}
          drafts={drafts}
          cellErrors={cellErrors}
          onCellChange={handleCellChange}
          search={search}
          page={page}
          onPageChange={setPage}
        />
      )}
    </main>
  );
}

export default EvaluatePage;
How the pieces work together:

Drafts: the page keeps one object with only the cells you've changed, keyed by student and parameter. The saved values come from the server. Because drafts live in the page and not in the grid, they survive searching and paging. A change that puts a cell back to its saved value removes it from the drafts.
Save sends only the changed cells, in one request. The server saves all or none. If it refuses, its list of problems is turned into red messages under the exact cells, and your drafts stay so nothing is lost.
await queryClient.invalidateQueries(...) inside onSuccess makes the button wait until the fresh saved values have arrived, and only then clears the drafts. Without the wait, you'd see the old numbers flash for a moment.
Switching period discards drafts after a confirm. Refreshing or closing the tab with unsaved changes triggers the browser's own warning (beforeunload).
The hooks all sit above the early returns, which is a React rule: hooks must always run in the same order.

H. Run and test

Start the server and client. The anchor from piece 13 (about 12 periods ago) works well here.

Evaluate appears in the menu. The page opens on the current period, with a dropdown and Previous/Next.
The grid shows your students down the side and the parameters that period uses across the top, each with its range. For the current period, that's Speed and Balance (Rhythm was removed from Period 10 on).
Type numbers into a few cells. They turn yellow, and the button says "Save 3 changes."
Type 15 in a Speed cell. A red message appears under it, and Save is disabled with "Fix the red cells before saving." Correct it.
Press Save. You get "Saved. 3 values changed.", the yellow disappears, and the numbers stay. Refresh the page and they're still there.
Change one value, save ("1 value changed"). Then delete the text from a cell and save, which clears it. Check it in the terminal (use your current period number):
curl -s "http://localhost:3000/api/evaluations?periodNumber=12"
Pick Period 3. The columns are now Speed and Rhythm, with no Balance. If you have unsaved edits, a confirm box appears first.
Type in a cell, then search for a student name and clear the search. Your yellow edit is still there.
Type in a cell and press refresh. The browser asks whether to leave.
Stop the server, edit a cell, and Save. You get a red error and your edit stays. Restart the server and Save again, which now works.
Leave the scores you saved. The next pieces need data to show. Note that the Settings page will now refuse to change the start date, because scores exist. That's the lock working. When you're ready to use the app for real, delete the test scores first with psql -h localhost -U stu_manager -d stu_database -c "DELETE FROM evaluations;", then set the real start date.

If something goes wrong

"No parameters are used in this period": that period's list is empty, so check the change history on the Parameters page.
A red "Set the period anchor date first" or a "not started yet" message on Save: check the Settings page.
A red message under a cell after Save: that's the server's own reason, so read it.
The grid never finishes loading: the server isn't running, or a request failed. Open F12, then the Network tab, and send me the failing request.
Import errors: a filename or path is misspelled, and the console names it.

Steps left: 4 pieces (17 to 20), counting this one.

Piece 17: parameters for this period
This adds a panel to the Evaluate page where you switch a parameter on or off for one period, or from that period onward. Before hiding a parameter that already has saved scores, it warns you how many are affected. The scores are never deleted, only hidden from the entry grid.

The count comes from the server, using the same tested rule that decides which parameters a period uses, so there's a single source of truth. This piece has a server part (with tests) and a client part.

Server part
cd /data/projects/student-panel/server
A. Two new functions

Add these to the end of src/lib/parameterUse.js:

js
export function withChange(changes, change) {
  const others = changes.filter(
    (existing) =>
      !(
        existing.parameterId === change.parameterId &&
        existing.periodNumber === change.periodNumber &&
        existing.scope === change.scope
      )
  );
  return [...others, change];
}

export function hiddenScores({ changes, change, scoreCounts }) {
  const after = withChange(changes, change);
  let affectedScores = 0;
  const periods = [];

  for (const { periodNumber, count } of scoreCounts) {
    const usedBefore = parametersUsedInPeriod(changes, periodNumber).includes(
      change.parameterId
    );
    const usedAfter = parametersUsedInPeriod(after, periodNumber).includes(
      change.parameterId
    );
    if (usedBefore && !usedAfter) {
      affectedScores += count;
      periods.push(periodNumber);
    }
  }
  return { affectedScores, periods };
}
withChange gives the change log as it would look after the new change. It mirrors the server's "same parameter, period, and scope replaces the old one" rule.
hiddenScores takes the changes for one parameter, the proposed change, and how many scores the parameter has in each period. For each period, it asks: was the parameter used before, and is it unused after? If so, those scores get hidden. Comparing before and after means we never have to reason about which periods a change reaches. The rule does it for us.
B. Tests

Create test/hiddenScores.test.js:

js
import { test } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { hiddenScores, withChange } from '../src/lib/parameterUse.js';

const start = {
  parameterId: 'p',
  periodNumber: 1,
  scope: 'from_here_on',
  action: 'add',
};
const counts = [
  { periodNumber: 4, count: 2 },
  { periodNumber: 6, count: 3 },
  { periodNumber: 8, count: 1 },
];

function remove(periodNumber, scope) {
  return { parameterId: 'p', periodNumber, scope, action: 'remove' };
}

test('removing from a period on hides scores from that period onward', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(
    hiddenScores({
      changes: [start],
      change: remove(6, 'from_here_on'),
      scoreCounts: counts,
    }),
    { affectedScores: 4, periods: [6, 8] }
  );
});

test('removing for one period only hides just that period', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(
    hiddenScores({
      changes: [start],
      change: remove(6, 'this_period_only'),
      scoreCounts: counts,
    }),
    { affectedScores: 3, periods: [6] }
  );
});

test('adding never hides scores', () => {
  const add = {
    parameterId: 'p',
    periodNumber: 3,
    scope: 'this_period_only',
    action: 'add',
  };
  assert.deepEqual(
    hiddenScores({ changes: [start], change: add, scoreCounts: counts }),
    { affectedScores: 0, periods: [] }
  );
});

test('nothing is hidden when the parameter is already unused there', () => {
  const changes = [start, remove(5, 'from_here_on')];
  assert.deepEqual(
    hiddenScores({
      changes,
      change: remove(7, 'from_here_on'),
      scoreCounts: [{ periodNumber: 8, count: 4 }],
    }),
    { affectedScores: 0, periods: [] }
  );
});

test('a later re-add keeps its periods visible', () => {
  const readd = {
    parameterId: 'p',
    periodNumber: 8,
    scope: 'from_here_on',
    action: 'add',
  };
  assert.deepEqual(
    hiddenScores({
      changes: [start, readd],
      change: remove(6, 'from_here_on'),
      scoreCounts: counts,
    }),
    { affectedScores: 3, periods: [6] }
  );
});

test('a new change replaces an existing one with the same period and scope', () => {
  const existing = remove(6, 'from_here_on');
  const add = {
    parameterId: 'p',
    periodNumber: 6,
    scope: 'from_here_on',
    action: 'add',
  };
  assert.deepEqual(withChange([start, existing], add), [start, add]);
  assert.deepEqual(
    hiddenScores({
      changes: [start, existing],
      change: add,
      scoreCounts: counts,
    }),
    { affectedScores: 0, periods: [] }
  );
});
npm test
You should see 6 new green tests, for a total of pass 29 and fail 0 (23 + 6). If any fail, send me the output before going on.

C. The route

In src/routes/parameters.js, replace the existing import from '../lib/parameterUse.js' at the top with this, and add the isUuid import line below it:

js
import {
  MAX_PERIOD,
  hiddenScores,
  isValidPeriodNumber,
  parametersUsedInPeriod,
} from '../lib/parameterUse.js';
import { isUuid } from '../lib/ids.js';
Then add this route right after the existing router.get('/parameter-changes', ...) block:

js
router.get('/parameter-changes/impact', async (req, res) => {
  const { parameterId, periodNumber, scope, action } = req.query;

  if (!isUuid(parameterId)) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'parameterId must be a valid ID.' });
  }
  const isDigits =
    typeof periodNumber === 'string' && /^\d+$/.test(periodNumber);
  if (!isDigits || !isValidPeriodNumber(Number(periodNumber))) {
    return res.status(400).json({
      error: `periodNumber must be a whole number from 1 to ${MAX_PERIOD}.`,
    });
  }
  if (!SCOPES.includes(scope)) {
    return res
      .status(400)
      .json({ error: `scope must be one of: ${SCOPES.join(', ')}.` });
  }
  if (!ACTIONS.includes(action)) {
    return res
      .status(400)
      .json({ error: `action must be one of: ${ACTIONS.join(', ')}.` });
  }

  const [changesResult, countsResult] = await Promise.all([
    pool.query(
      `SELECT parameter_id  AS "parameterId",
              period_number AS "periodNumber",
              scope,
              action
       FROM parameter_changes
       WHERE parameter_id = $1`,
      [parameterId]
    ),
    pool.query(
      `SELECT period_number AS "periodNumber", count(*)::int AS count
       FROM evaluations
       WHERE parameter_id = $1 AND value IS NOT NULL
       GROUP BY period_number
       ORDER BY period_number`,
      [parameterId]
    ),
  ]);

  res.json(
    hiddenScores({
      changes: changesResult.rows,
      change: {
        parameterId,
        periodNumber: Number(periodNumber),
        scope,
        action,
      },
      scoreCounts: countsResult.rows,
    })
  );
});
It doesn't change anything. It just answers "if you made this change, how many saved scores would disappear from the grid, and in which periods?" The server restarts on save.

Client part
cd /data/projects/student-panel/client
D. API functions

Add these to the end of src/api/parameters.js:

js
export function createParameterChange(change) {
  return request('/api/parameter-changes', {
    method: 'POST',
    body: JSON.stringify(change),
  });
}

export function getChangeImpact({ parameterId, periodNumber, scope, action }) {
  const params = new URLSearchParams({
    parameterId,
    periodNumber: String(periodNumber),
    scope,
    action,
  });
  return request(`/api/parameter-changes/impact?${params}`);
}
E. The panel

Create src/components/PeriodParametersPanel.jsx:

jsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import { useMutation, useQuery, useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { listPeriodParameters } from '../api/evaluations.js';
import {
  createParameterChange,
  getChangeImpact,
  listParameters,
} from '../api/parameters.js';

function ParameterChoice({ parameter, used, periodNumber, disabled }) {
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
  const action = used ? 'remove' : 'add';
  const [isOpen, setIsOpen] = useState(false);
  const [scope, setScope] = useState('this_period_only');
  const [checking, setChecking] = useState(false);
  const [problem, setProblem] = useState(null);

  const change = useMutation({
    mutationFn: createParameterChange,
    onSuccess: () => {
      setIsOpen(false);
      queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['period-parameters'] });
      queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['parameter-changes'] });
    },
  });

  async function handleConfirm() {
    setProblem(null);
    const request = { parameterId: parameter.id, periodNumber, scope, action };

    if (action === 'remove') {
      setChecking(true);
      try {
        const impact = await getChangeImpact(request);
        if (impact.affectedScores > 0) {
          const ok = window.confirm(
            `${impact.affectedScores} saved score(s) for ${parameter.name} ` +
              `(period ${impact.periods.join(', ')}) will no longer appear in ` +
              'the entry grid. They are kept, not deleted, and come back if ' +
              'you undo this change. Continue?'
          );
          if (!ok) return;
        }
      } catch (err) {
        setProblem(err.message);
        return;
      } finally {
        setChecking(false);
      }
    }
    change.mutate(request);
  }

  return (
    <li className="param-choice">
      <div className="param-line">
        <span>
          <strong>{parameter.name}</strong>: {used ? 'used' : 'not used'} in
          Period {periodNumber}
        </span>
        {!isOpen && (
          <button
            type="button"
            onClick={() => setIsOpen(true)}
            disabled={disabled}
          >
            {used ? 'Remove…' : 'Add…'}
          </button>
        )}
      </div>

      {isOpen && (
        <div className="param-form">
          <label>
            <input
              type="radio"
              name={`scope-${parameter.id}`}
              checked={scope === 'this_period_only'}
              onChange={() => setScope('this_period_only')}
            />
            This period only (Period {periodNumber})
          </label>
          <label>
            <input
              type="radio"
              name={`scope-${parameter.id}`}
              checked={scope === 'from_here_on'}
              onChange={() => setScope('from_here_on')}
            />
            Period {periodNumber} and all following periods
          </label>
          <div className="row">
            <button
              type="button"
              onClick={handleConfirm}
              disabled={checking || change.isPending}
            >
              {used ? 'Remove' : 'Add'}
            </button>
            <button type="button" onClick={() => setIsOpen(false)}>
              Cancel
            </button>
          </div>
        </div>
      )}

      {problem && <p className="error">{problem}</p>}
      {change.isError && <p className="error">{change.error.message}</p>}
    </li>
  );
}

function PeriodParametersPanel({ periodNumber, disabled }) {
  const catalog = useQuery({
    queryKey: ['parameters'],
    queryFn: listParameters,
  });
  const inUse = useQuery({
    queryKey: ['period-parameters', periodNumber],
    queryFn: () => listPeriodParameters(periodNumber),
  });

  if (catalog.isPending || inUse.isPending) return <p>Loading parameters...</p>;
  const failure = catalog.error ?? inUse.error;
  if (failure) return <p className="error">{failure.message}</p>;

  const usedIds = new Set(inUse.data.map((parameter) => parameter.id));

  return (
    <details className="panel">
      <summary>
        Parameters for Period {periodNumber} ({inUse.data.length} used)
      </summary>
      <p className="hint">
        "This period only" affects just this period. "All following periods"
        applies from here until the next change to that parameter. Saved scores
        are never deleted.
      </p>
      {disabled && (
        <p className="hint">
          Save or discard your unsaved changes before changing parameters.
        </p>
      )}
      <ul className="plain-list">
        {catalog.data.map((parameter) => (
          <ParameterChoice
            key={`${parameter.id}:${periodNumber}`}
            parameter={parameter}
            used={usedIds.has(parameter.id)}
            periodNumber={periodNumber}
            disabled={disabled}
          />
        ))}
      </ul>
    </details>
  );
}

export default PeriodParametersPanel;
Each parameter shows whether the period uses it, and one button: "Remove…" if it's used, "Add…" if not. The button opens a small choice: this period only, or this period and all following.
Remove first asks the server for the impact. If saved scores would be hidden, you get the confirm box with the count and periods. Cancel and nothing changes. Add never hides anything, so it goes straight through.
checking disables the button while the impact question is in flight, so you can't double-submit.
The panel shares its data with the grid through the same query key (['period-parameters', periodNumber]), so a change refreshes both at once.
key={parameter.id:periodNumber} gives each row a fresh state when you switch period, so a half-open form doesn't carry over.
It's disabled while you have unsaved edits. Changing the columns under a half-typed grid would leave those drafts pointing at cells that no longer exist.

Piece 18: the student page and its history grid
Clicking a student in the list opens a page with their whole history as one grid, periods down and parameters across. It's the overview from your design, and pieces 19's two views will slice the same data.

The grid tells apart two kinds of blank cell:

"—" means the parameter was in use that period but nobody measured it.
grey "n/a" means the parameter wasn't part of that period at all.
It needs two small server changes first: a route to fetch one student, and each period in /api/periods now lists which parameters it uses.

Server part
cd /data/projects/student-panel/server
A. Fetch one student

In src/routes/students.js, add this just above export default router;:

js
router.get('/:id', async (req, res) => {
  const result = await pool.query(
    `SELECT ${STUDENT_COLUMNS}
     FROM students
     WHERE id = $1 AND deleted_at IS NULL`,
    [req.params.id]
  );
  if (result.rowCount === 0) {
    return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Student not found.' });
  }
  res.json(result.rows[0]);
});
The existing router.param('id', ...) check answers 404 for anything that isn't a proper ID, so this route only sees valid ones.

B. Which parameters each period uses

In src/routes/periods.js, add this import with the others:

js
import { parametersUsedInPeriod } from '../lib/parameterUse.js';
Then replace the whole router.get('/periods', ...) block with:

js
router.get('/periods', async (req, res) => {
  const today = todayIso();
  const anchorDate = await readAnchor();
  if (!anchorDate) {
    return res.json({ anchorDate: null, today, current: null, periods: [] });
  }

  const changesResult = await pool.query(
    `SELECT parameter_id  AS "parameterId",
            period_number AS "periodNumber",
            scope,
            action
     FROM parameter_changes`
  );

  const current = periodForDate(anchorDate, today);
  const periods = [];
  for (let number = 1; number <= (current ?? 0); number++) {
    periods.push({
      ...periodDates(anchorDate, number),
      parameterIds: parametersUsedInPeriod(changesResult.rows, number),
    });
  }
  res.json({ anchorDate, today, current, periods });
});
Each period now carries parameterIds, computed by the same tested rule as before. The screens that already use /api/periods ignore the extra field, so nothing else breaks.

E. The reshaping logic, with tests

The database gives a flat list of scores. The grid needs a table. This conversion is the risky part, so it gets its own tested file. Create src/lib/history.js:

js
export function buildHistory({ evaluations, periods, parameters }) {
  const valueByCell = new Map(
    evaluations.map((item) => [
      `${item.periodNumber}:${item.parameterId}`,
      item.value,
    ])
  );
  const scoredIds = new Set(evaluations.map((item) => item.parameterId));
  const usedAnywhere = new Set(periods.flatMap((period) => period.parameterIds));

  const columns = parameters.filter(
    (parameter) => usedAnywhere.has(parameter.id) || scoredIds.has(parameter.id)
  );

  const rows = periods.map((period) => {
    const inUse = new Set(period.parameterIds);
    const cells = {};
    for (const parameter of columns) {
      const key = `${period.number}:${parameter.id}`;
      if (valueByCell.has(key)) {
        cells[parameter.id] = { state: 'value', value: valueByCell.get(key) };
      } else if (inUse.has(parameter.id)) {
        cells[parameter.id] = { state: 'missing' };
      } else {
        cells[parameter.id] = { state: 'unused' };
      }
    }
    return {
      periodNumber: period.number,
      startDate: period.startDate,
      endDate: period.endDate,
      cells,
    };
  });

  return { columns, rows };
}

export function visibleRows(rows, evaluations, showAll) {
  if (showAll || rows.length === 0) return rows;

  const scored = evaluations.map((item) => item.periodNumber);
  const first =
    scored.length > 0
      ? Math.min(...scored)
      : rows[rows.length - 1].periodNumber;
  return rows.filter((row) => row.periodNumber >= first);
}
buildHistory turns the flat list into columns (parameters) and rows (periods, in order). Each cell is value, missing (in use but not measured), or unused (not part of that period).
A parameter gets a column if any period uses it or the student has a score for it. So scores hidden by a later change still show up, and a parameter nobody ever used doesn't clutter the table.
visibleRows hides the periods before the student's first score, because "not measured" for weeks before they existed would only be noise. If there are no scores at all, it shows only the current period. "Show all periods" reveals everything.
Create test/history.test.js:

js
import { test } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { buildHistory, visibleRows } from '../src/lib/history.js';

const speed = { id: 'speed', name: 'Speed' };
const balance = { id: 'balance', name: 'Balance' };
const neverUsed = { id: 'unused', name: 'Never used' };
const parameters = [speed, balance, neverUsed];

const periods = [
  { number: 1, startDate: '2026-09-19', endDate: '2026-09-25', parameterIds: ['speed'] },
  { number: 2, startDate: '2026-09-26', endDate: '2026-10-02', parameterIds: ['speed'] },
  {
    number: 3,
    startDate: '2026-10-03',
    endDate: '2026-10-09',
    parameterIds: ['speed', 'balance'],
  },
];

test('cells are values, missing (in use but not measured), or unused', () => {
  const evaluations = [
    { periodNumber: 1, parameterId: 'speed', value: 7 },
    { periodNumber: 3, parameterId: 'speed', value: 8 },
  ];
  const { columns, rows } = buildHistory({ evaluations, periods, parameters });

  assert.deepEqual(
    columns.map((column) => column.id),
    ['speed', 'balance']
  );
  assert.deepEqual(
    rows.map((row) => row.cells.speed),
    [{ state: 'value', value: 7 }, { state: 'missing' }, { state: 'value', value: 8 }]
  );
  assert.deepEqual(
    rows.map((row) => row.cells.balance.state),
    ['unused', 'unused', 'missing']
  );
});

test('a scored parameter that no period uses still gets a column', () => {
  const evaluations = [{ periodNumber: 2, parameterId: 'unused', value: 5 }];
  const { columns, rows } = buildHistory({ evaluations, periods, parameters });

  assert.deepEqual(
    columns.map((column) => column.id),
    ['speed', 'balance', 'unused']
  );
  assert.deepEqual(
    rows.map((row) => row.cells.unused.state),
    ['unused', 'value', 'unused']
  );
});

test('parameters that are never used and never scored are left out', () => {
  const { columns } = buildHistory({ evaluations: [], periods, parameters });
  assert.deepEqual(
    columns.map((column) => column.id),
    ['speed', 'balance']
  );
});

test('rows start at the first scored period unless everything is requested', () => {
  const evaluations = [{ periodNumber: 2, parameterId: 'speed', value: 5 }];
  const { rows } = buildHistory({ evaluations, periods, parameters });

  assert.deepEqual(
    visibleRows(rows, evaluations, false).map((row) => row.periodNumber),
    [2, 3]
  );
  assert.deepEqual(
    visibleRows(rows, evaluations, true).map((row) => row.periodNumber),
    [1, 2, 3]
  );
});

test('with no scores only the current period is shown', () => {
  const { rows } = buildHistory({ evaluations: [], periods, parameters });
  assert.deepEqual(
    visibleRows(rows, [], false).map((row) => row.periodNumber),
    [3]
  );
  assert.deepEqual(visibleRows([], [], false), []);
});

F. Formatting helpers

Add these to the end of src/lib/format.js:

js
export function formatShortIsoDate(isoDate) {
  const [year, month, day] = isoDate.split('-').map(Number);
  return new Date(year, month - 1, day).toLocaleDateString(undefined, {
    day: 'numeric',
    month: 'short',
  });
}

export function formatParameterHint(parameter) {
  const range = formatRange(parameter);
  return [range === '—' ? null : range, parameter.unit]
    .filter(Boolean)
    .join(' · ');
}
G. The grid and the page

Create src/components/HistoryGrid.jsx:

jsx
import { useMemo, useState } from 'react';
import { formatParameterHint, formatShortIsoDate } from '../lib/format.js';
import { buildHistory, visibleRows } from '../lib/history.js';

function Cell({ cell }) {
  if (cell.state === 'value') {
    return <td className="num">{cell.value}</td>;
  }
  if (cell.state === 'missing') {
    return (
      <td className="num muted" title="In use, but not measured">
        —
      </td>
    );
  }
  return (
    <td className="num na" title="Not used in this period">
      n/a
    </td>
  );
}

function HistoryGrid({ evaluations, periods, parameters }) {
  const [showAll, setShowAll] = useState(false);

  const history = useMemo(
    () => buildHistory({ evaluations, periods, parameters }),
    [evaluations, periods, parameters]
  );
  const rows = useMemo(
    () => [...visibleRows(history.rows, evaluations, showAll)].reverse(),
    [history.rows, evaluations, showAll]
  );

  if (history.columns.length === 0) {
    return <p>No parameters are in use yet. Add some on the Parameters page.</p>;
  }
  if (rows.length === 0) return <p>No periods have started yet.</p>;

  return (
    <>
      <label className="inline">
        <input
          type="checkbox"
          checked={showAll}
          onChange={(event) => setShowAll(event.target.checked)}
        />
        Show all periods
      </label>
      <p className="hint">
        Periods before the first score are hidden. "—" means the parameter was
        in use but not measured. "n/a" means it was not used that period.
      </p>

      <div className="table-wrap">
        <table className="history">
          <thead>
            <tr>
              <th>Period</th>
              {history.columns.map((parameter) => (
                <th key={parameter.id} className="num">
                  {parameter.name}
                  <div className="hint">{formatParameterHint(parameter)}</div>
                </th>
              ))}
            </tr>
          </thead>
          <tbody>
            {rows.map((row) => (
              <tr key={row.periodNumber}>
                <th scope="row">
                  Period {row.periodNumber}
                  <div className="hint">
                    {formatShortIsoDate(row.startDate)} –{' '}
                    {formatShortIsoDate(row.endDate)}
                  </div>
                </th>
                {history.columns.map((parameter) => (
                  <Cell key={parameter.id} cell={row.cells[parameter.id]} />
                ))}
              </tr>
            ))}
          </tbody>
        </table>
      </div>
    </>
  );
}

export default HistoryGrid;
useMemo remembers a calculated result and only recomputes it when its inputs change. Reshaping the history is cheap, but there's no reason to redo it on every keystroke or redraw. The rows are shown newest first (.reverse() on a copy).

Create src/pages/StudentDetailPage.jsx:

jsx
import { useQuery } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { Link, useParams } from 'react-router';
import { listStudentEvaluations } from '../api/evaluations.js';
import { listParameters } from '../api/parameters.js';
import { getStudent } from '../api/students.js';
import HistoryGrid from '../components/HistoryGrid.jsx';
import { formatStudentCode } from '../lib/format.js';
import { usePeriods } from '../lib/usePeriods.js';

function StudentDetailPage() {
  const { id } = useParams();

  const studentQuery = useQuery({
    queryKey: ['student', id],
    queryFn: () => getStudent(id),
  });
  const evaluationsQuery = useQuery({
    queryKey: ['evaluations', { studentId: id }],
    queryFn: () => listStudentEvaluations(id),
  });
  const parametersQuery = useQuery({
    queryKey: ['parameters'],
    queryFn: listParameters,
  });
  const periodsQuery = usePeriods();

  const backLink = (
    <p>
      <Link to="/students">← Back to students</Link>
    </p>
  );

  if (studentQuery.isError && studentQuery.error.status === 404) {
    return (
      <main>
        {backLink}
        <h1>Student not found</h1>
        <p>This student does not exist or was deleted.</p>
      </main>
    );
  }

  const queries = [
    studentQuery,
    evaluationsQuery,
    parametersQuery,
    periodsQuery,
  ];
  const failed = queries.find((query) => query.isError);
  if (failed) {
    return (
      <main>
        {backLink}
        <p className="error">{failed.error.message}</p>
      </main>
    );
  }
  if (queries.some((query) => query.isPending)) {
    return (
      <main>
        {backLink}
        <p>Loading...</p>
      </main>
    );
  }

  const student = studentQuery.data;
  const periods = periodsQuery.data;

  return (
    <main className="wide">
      {backLink}
      <h1>{student.fullName}</h1>
      <p className="hint code">Code {formatStudentCode(student.studentCode)}</p>

      <section>
        <h2>History</h2>
        {!periods.anchorDate ? (
          <p>
            Set the period start date first, on the{' '}
            <Link to="/settings">Settings</Link> page.
          </p>
        ) : (
          <HistoryGrid
            evaluations={evaluationsQuery.data}
            periods={periods.periods}
            parameters={parametersQuery.data}
          />
        )}
      </section>
    </main>
  );
}

export default StudentDetailPage;
useParams() reads the :id part of the address (/students/3f2b...).
The scores use the key ['evaluations', { studentId: id }]. Saving on the Evaluate page refreshes everything starting with ['evaluations'], so this page updates by itself.
All hooks come first, and the early returns come after them, as React requires.

        ) : (
          <Link to={`/students/${student.id}`}>{student.fullName}</Link>
        )}
