Steps left: 2 pieces (19 and 20), counting this one.

Piece 19: the By period and By parameter views
This adds two more tabs to the student page. They slice the same data as the Overview grid, so no server changes are needed.

By period shows one period: every parameter's score, a bar showing where it sits in that parameter's range, and the change from the previous period (▲ or ▼).
By parameter shows one parameter across time: a line chart, a summary line (latest, average, change since the first score), and a table.
A design choice: the By period bars are drawn per row, each scaled to that parameter's own range. One shared bar chart would put Speed (0 to 10) next to something like km/h on the same axis, which misleads. The line chart is the only place we use a chart library.

cd /data/projects/student-panel/client
A. Install the chart library

Recharts needs a react-is package whose version matches your React version, so we install both together:

npm install recharts react-is@$(node -p "require('react/package.json').version")
The second part reads your React's version and installs react-is at exactly that version.

B. The logic, with tests

Create src/lib/views.js:

js
function round(number) {
  return Math.round(number * 100) / 100;
}

export function percentOfRange(value, minValue, maxValue) {
  if (value === null || minValue === null || maxValue === null) return null;
  if (maxValue <= minValue) return null;
  const percent = ((value - minValue) * 100) / (maxValue - minValue);
  return Math.min(100, Math.max(0, percent));
}

export function periodView({ periodNumber, evaluations, periods, parameters }) {
  const period = periods.find((item) => item.number === periodNumber);
  const inUse = new Set(period?.parameterIds ?? []);
  const valueOf = new Map(
    evaluations.map((item) => [
      `${item.periodNumber}:${item.parameterId}`,
      item.value,
    ])
  );

  return parameters
    .filter(
      (parameter) =>
        inUse.has(parameter.id) || valueOf.has(`${periodNumber}:${parameter.id}`)
    )
    .map((parameter) => {
      const value = valueOf.get(`${periodNumber}:${parameter.id}`) ?? null;
      const previous =
        valueOf.get(`${periodNumber - 1}:${parameter.id}`) ?? null;
      return {
        parameter,
        inUse: inUse.has(parameter.id),
        value,
        previous,
        change:
          value !== null && previous !== null ? round(value - previous) : null,
        percent: percentOfRange(value, parameter.minValue, parameter.maxValue),
      };
    });
}

export function parameterView({ parameterId, evaluations, periods }) {
  const valueOf = new Map(
    evaluations
      .filter((item) => item.parameterId === parameterId)
      .map((item) => [item.periodNumber, item.value])
  );
  if (valueOf.size === 0) return { points: [], stats: null };

  const firstScored = Math.min(...valueOf.keys());
  const points = periods
    .filter(
      (period) =>
        period.number >= firstScored &&
        (period.parameterIds.includes(parameterId) || valueOf.has(period.number))
    )
    .map((period) => ({
      periodNumber: period.number,
      startDate: period.startDate,
      endDate: period.endDate,
      value: valueOf.get(period.number) ?? null,
    }));

  const scored = points.filter((point) => point.value !== null);
  if (scored.length === 0) return { points, stats: null };

  const first = scored[0];
  const latest = scored[scored.length - 1];
  const sum = scored.reduce((total, point) => total + point.value, 0);

  return {
    points,
    stats: {
      count: scored.length,
      first: { periodNumber: first.periodNumber, value: first.value },
      latest: { periodNumber: latest.periodNumber, value: latest.value },
      average: round(sum / scored.length),
      change: scored.length > 1 ? round(latest.value - first.value) : null,
    },
  };
}
periodView lists the parameters a period uses, plus any parameter with a score in it (so a score hidden by a later change never vanishes). For each one it finds the value, the previous period's value, and the change. round avoids results like 0.19999999999999998.
parameterView builds the time series for one parameter. It starts at the parameter's first score, since earlier periods are noise. A period where the parameter was in use but nothing was entered gets value: null (a gap). A period where it wasn't in use, and has no score, is left out entirely.
percentOfRange gives how far a value sits between the parameter's minimum and maximum, or null if the parameter has no range.
Create test/views.test.js:

js
import { test } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { parameterView, percentOfRange, periodView } from '../src/lib/views.js';

const speed = { id: 'speed', name: 'Speed', minValue: 0, maxValue: 10 };
const balance = { id: 'balance', name: 'Balance', minValue: null, maxValue: null };
const old = { id: 'old', name: 'Old', minValue: null, maxValue: null };
const parameters = [speed, balance, old];

const periods = [
  { number: 1, startDate: '2026-09-19', endDate: '2026-09-25', parameterIds: ['speed'] },
  { number: 2, startDate: '2026-09-26', endDate: '2026-10-02', parameterIds: ['speed'] },
  {
    number: 3,
    startDate: '2026-10-03',
    endDate: '2026-10-09',
    parameterIds: ['speed', 'balance'],
  },
];

const evaluations = [
  { periodNumber: 1, parameterId: 'speed', value: 6 },
  { periodNumber: 2, parameterId: 'speed', value: 8 },
  { periodNumber: 3, parameterId: 'balance', value: 4 },
];

const summary = (rows) =>
  rows.map((r) => [r.parameter.id, r.value, r.previous, r.change, r.percent]);

test('percent of range, clamped and null when there is no range', () => {
  assert.equal(percentOfRange(5, 0, 10), 50);
  assert.equal(percentOfRange(11, 0, 10), 100);
  assert.equal(percentOfRange(-1, 0, 10), 0);
  assert.equal(percentOfRange(5, null, 10), null);
  assert.equal(percentOfRange(null, 0, 10), null);
  assert.equal(percentOfRange(5, 3, 3), null);
});

test('period view compares with the previous period', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(
    summary(periodView({ periodNumber: 1, evaluations, periods, parameters })),
    [['speed', 6, null, null, 60]]
  );
  assert.deepEqual(
    summary(periodView({ periodNumber: 2, evaluations, periods, parameters })),
    [['speed', 8, 6, 2, 80]]
  );
});

test('a parameter in use but not measured shows an empty value', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(
    summary(periodView({ periodNumber: 3, evaluations, periods, parameters })),
    [
      ['speed', null, 8, null, null],
      ['balance', 4, null, null, null],
    ]
  );
});

test('a score for a parameter no longer in use still appears, marked as not in use', () => {
  const withOld = [...evaluations, { periodNumber: 2, parameterId: 'old', value: 5 }];
  const rows = periodView({ periodNumber: 2, evaluations: withOld, periods, parameters });
  assert.deepEqual(
    rows.map((r) => [r.parameter.id, r.inUse, r.value]),
    [
      ['speed', true, 8],
      ['old', false, 5],
    ]
  );
});

test('changes are rounded to avoid floating point noise', () => {
  const values = [
    { periodNumber: 1, parameterId: 'balance', value: 0.1 },
    { periodNumber: 2, parameterId: 'balance', value: 0.3 },
  ];
  const usesBalance = periods.map((p) => ({ ...p, parameterIds: ['balance'] }));
  const rows = periodView({
    periodNumber: 2,
    evaluations: values,
    periods: usesBalance,
    parameters,
  });
  assert.equal(rows[0].change, 0.2);
});

test('parameter view has gaps for missing periods and summary numbers', () => {
  const scores = [
    { periodNumber: 1, parameterId: 'speed', value: 6 },
    { periodNumber: 3, parameterId: 'speed', value: 8 },
  ];
  const { points, stats } = parameterView({
    parameterId: 'speed',
    evaluations: scores,
    periods,
  });
  assert.deepEqual(
    points.map((p) => [p.periodNumber, p.value]),
    [
      [1, 6],
      [2, null],
      [3, 8],
    ]
  );
  assert.deepEqual(stats, {
    count: 2,
    first: { periodNumber: 1, value: 6 },
    latest: { periodNumber: 3, value: 8 },
    average: 7,
    change: 2,
  });
});

test('parameter view starts at the first score; one score has no change', () => {
  const { points, stats } = parameterView({
    parameterId: 'balance',
    evaluations,
    periods,
  });
  assert.deepEqual(
    points.map((p) => [p.periodNumber, p.value]),
    [[3, 4]]
  );
  assert.equal(stats.count, 1);
  assert.equal(stats.average, 4);
  assert.equal(stats.change, null);
});

test('parameter view keeps a scored period even if the parameter is no longer used there', () => {
  const nobodyUses = periods.map((p) => ({ ...p, parameterIds: [] }));
  const { points } = parameterView({
    parameterId: 'speed',
    evaluations: [{ periodNumber: 2, parameterId: 'speed', value: 5 }],
    periods: nobodyUses,
  });
  assert.deepEqual(
    points.map((p) => [p.periodNumber, p.value]),
    [[2, 5]]
  );
});

test('parameter view with no scores is empty', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(
    parameterView({ parameterId: 'old', evaluations, periods }),
    { points: [], stats: null }
  );
});
Run:

npm test
You should see pass 19 and fail 0 (11 + 8 new). If anything fails, send me the output before continuing.

C. Small formatter

Add to the end of src/lib/format.js:

js
export function formatChange(change) {
  if (change === null) return '—';
  if (change === 0) return '0';
  return `${change > 0 ? '▲' : '▼'} ${Math.abs(change)}`;
}
D. The By period view

Create src/components/ByPeriodView.jsx:

jsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import { formatChange } from '../lib/format.js';
import { periodView } from '../lib/views.js';
import PeriodPicker from './PeriodPicker.jsx';

function Meter({ percent }) {
  if (percent === null) return null;
  return (
    <div className="meter" aria-hidden="true">
      <div className="meter-fill" style={{ width: `${percent}%` }} />
    </div>
  );
}

function ByPeriodView({ evaluations, periods, parameters }) {
  const [chosen, setChosen] = useState(null);

  const scoredPeriods = evaluations.map((item) => item.periodNumber);
  const defaultPeriod =
    scoredPeriods.length > 0 ? Math.max(...scoredPeriods) : periods.current;
  const periodNumber = chosen ?? defaultPeriod;

  const rows = periodView({
    periodNumber,
    evaluations,
    periods: periods.periods,
    parameters,
  });

  return (
    <>
      <PeriodPicker periods={periods} value={periodNumber} onChange={setChosen} />

      {rows.length === 0 ? (
        <p>
          No parameters were used in Period {periodNumber}, and no scores were
          entered.
        </p>
      ) : (
        <div className="table-wrap">
          <table>
            <thead>
              <tr>
                <th>Parameter</th>
                <th className="num">Score</th>
                <th>Within range</th>
                <th className="num">Change vs previous period</th>
              </tr>
            </thead>
            <tbody>
              {rows.map((row) => (
                <tr key={row.parameter.id}>
                  <td>
                    {row.parameter.name}
                    {!row.inUse && (
                      <span className="hint"> (not used this period)</span>
                    )}
                  </td>
                  <td className="num">{row.value === null ? '—' : row.value}</td>
                  <td>
                    <Meter percent={row.percent} />
                  </td>
                  <td className="num">{formatChange(row.change)}</td>
                </tr>
              ))}
            </tbody>
          </table>
        </div>
      )}
    </>
  );
}

export default ByPeriodView;
It opens on the student's latest scored period, or the current one if there are none. It reuses the PeriodPicker from the Evaluate page.

E. The By parameter view

Create src/components/ByParameterView.jsx:

jsx
import { useMemo, useState } from 'react';
import {
  CartesianGrid,
  Line,
  LineChart,
  ResponsiveContainer,
  Tooltip,
  XAxis,
  YAxis,
} from 'recharts';
import { formatChange, formatShortIsoDate } from '../lib/format.js';
import { buildHistory } from '../lib/history.js';
import { parameterView } from '../lib/views.js';

function ByParameterView({ evaluations, periods, parameters }) {
  const [chosenId, setChosenId] = useState(null);

  const list = periods.periods;
  const columns = useMemo(
    () => buildHistory({ evaluations, periods: list, parameters }).columns,
    [evaluations, list, parameters]
  );

  if (columns.length === 0) {
    return <p>No parameters are in use yet. Add some on the Parameters page.</p>;
  }

  const inUseNow = new Set(list[list.length - 1]?.parameterIds ?? []);
  const scoredIds = new Set(evaluations.map((item) => item.parameterId));
  const defaultId = (columns.find((c) => scoredIds.has(c.id)) ?? columns[0]).id;
  const parameterId = columns.some((c) => c.id === chosenId)
    ? chosenId
    : defaultId;
  const parameter = columns.find((c) => c.id === parameterId);

  const { points, stats } = parameterView({
    parameterId,
    evaluations,
    periods: list,
  });
  const chartData = points.map((point) => ({
    label: `P${point.periodNumber}`,
    value: point.value,
  }));

  return (
    <>
      <label className="inline">
        Parameter
        <select
          value={parameterId}
          onChange={(event) => setChosenId(event.target.value)}
        >
          {columns.map((column) => (
            <option key={column.id} value={column.id}>
              {column.name}
              {inUseNow.has(column.id) ? '' : ' (not in use now)'}
            </option>
          ))}
        </select>
      </label>

      {!stats ? (
        <p>No scores for {parameter.name} yet.</p>
      ) : (
        <>
          <p className="summary">
            Latest <strong>{stats.latest.value}</strong> (Period{' '}
            {stats.latest.periodNumber}) · Average{' '}
            <strong>{stats.average}</strong>
            {stats.change !== null && (
              <>
                {' '}
                · Since first score <strong>{formatChange(stats.change)}</strong>
              </>
            )}{' '}
            · {stats.count} score{stats.count === 1 ? '' : 's'}
          </p>

          <div className="chart-box">
            <ResponsiveContainer width="100%" height={260}>
              <LineChart
                data={chartData}
                margin={{ top: 8, right: 16, bottom: 0, left: 0 }}
              >
                <CartesianGrid
                  stroke="rgba(128,128,128,0.3)"
                  strokeDasharray="3 3"
                />
                <XAxis
                  dataKey="label"
                  tick={{ fill: 'currentColor', fontSize: 12 }}
                />
                <YAxis
                  domain={[parameter.minValue ?? 'auto', parameter.maxValue ?? 'auto']}
                  tick={{ fill: 'currentColor', fontSize: 12 }}
                  width={40}
                />
                <Tooltip />
                <Line
                  type="linear"
                  dataKey="value"
                  name={parameter.name}
                  stroke="#4f46e5"
                  strokeWidth={2}
                  dot={{ r: 3 }}
                  connectNulls={false}
                />
              </LineChart>
            </ResponsiveContainer>
          </div>

          <div className="table-wrap">
            <table>
              <thead>
                <tr>
                  <th>Period</th>
                  <th>Dates</th>
                  <th className="num">Score</th>
                </tr>
              </thead>
              <tbody>
                {[...points].reverse().map((point) => (
                  <tr key={point.periodNumber}>
                    <td>Period {point.periodNumber}</td>
                    <td>
                      {formatShortIsoDate(point.startDate)} –{' '}
                      {formatShortIsoDate(point.endDate)}
                    </td>
                    <td className="num">
                      {point.value === null ? '—' : point.value}
                    </td>
                  </tr>
                ))}
              </tbody>
            </table>
          </div>
        </>
      )}
    </>
  );
}

export default ByParameterView;
LineChart is the frame. CartesianGrid, XAxis, YAxis, Tooltip, and Line are the parts placed inside it. ResponsiveContainer makes it fill the width of the page.
A value: null leaves a gap in the line, and connectNulls={false} keeps it that way, so a missed week can't be mistaken for a smooth trend.
The chart's Y axis uses the parameter's own minimum and maximum when it has them, and 'auto' otherwise. Text uses currentColor, so it follows your light or dark theme.
The picker lists every parameter the student has a score for or that was ever in use. A parameter no longer in the current period is labelled "(not in use now)", but its history stays viewable.
F. Tabs on the student page

Replace all of src/pages/StudentDetailPage.jsx with:

jsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import { useQuery } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { Link, useParams } from 'react-router';
import { listStudentEvaluations } from '../api/evaluations.js';
import { listParameters } from '../api/parameters.js';
import { getStudent } from '../api/students.js';
import ByParameterView from '../components/ByParameterView.jsx';
import ByPeriodView from '../components/ByPeriodView.jsx';
import HistoryGrid from '../components/HistoryGrid.jsx';
import { formatStudentCode } from '../lib/format.js';
import { usePeriods } from '../lib/usePeriods.js';

const TABS = [
  { id: 'overview', label: 'Overview' },
  { id: 'period', label: 'By period' },
  { id: 'parameter', label: 'By parameter' },
];

function StudentDetailPage() {
  const { id } = useParams();
  const [tab, setTab] = useState('overview');

  const studentQuery = useQuery({
    queryKey: ['student', id],
    queryFn: () => getStudent(id),
  });
  const evaluationsQuery = useQuery({
    queryKey: ['evaluations', { studentId: id }],
    queryFn: () => listStudentEvaluations(id),
  });
  const parametersQuery = useQuery({
    queryKey: ['parameters'],
    queryFn: listParameters,
  });
  const periodsQuery = usePeriods();

  const backLink = (
    <p>
      <Link to="/students">← Back to students</Link>
    </p>
  );

  if (studentQuery.isError && studentQuery.error.status === 404) {
    return (
      <main>
        {backLink}
        <h1>Student not found</h1>
        <p>This student does not exist or was deleted.</p>
      </main>
    );
  }

  const queries = [
    studentQuery,
    evaluationsQuery,
    parametersQuery,
    periodsQuery,
  ];
  const failed = queries.find((query) => query.isError);
  if (failed) {
    return (
      <main>
        {backLink}
        <p className="error">{failed.error.message}</p>
      </main>
    );
  }
  if (queries.some((query) => query.isPending)) {
    return (
      <main>
        {backLink}
        <p>Loading...</p>
      </main>
    );
  }

  const student = studentQuery.data;
  const periods = periodsQuery.data;
  const evaluations = evaluationsQuery.data;
  const parameters = parametersQuery.data;

  let content;
  if (!periods.anchorDate) {
    content = (
      <p>
        Set the period start date first, on the{' '}
        <Link to="/settings">Settings</Link> page.
      </p>
    );
  } else if (periods.current === null) {
    content = <p>Period 1 has not started yet.</p>;
  } else if (tab === 'period') {
    content = (
      <ByPeriodView
        evaluations={evaluations}
        periods={periods}
        parameters={parameters}
      />
    );
  } else if (tab === 'parameter') {
    content = (
      <ByParameterView
        evaluations={evaluations}
        periods={periods}
        parameters={parameters}
      />
    );
  } else {
    content = (
      <HistoryGrid
        evaluations={evaluations}
        periods={periods.periods}
        parameters={parameters}
      />
    );
  }

  return (
    <main className="wide">
      {backLink}
      <h1>{student.fullName}</h1>
      <p className="hint code">Code {formatStudentCode(student.studentCode)}</p>

      <section>
        <h2>History</h2>
        <div className="tabs" role="tablist" aria-label="History views">
          {TABS.map((item) => (
            <button
              key={item.id}
              type="button"
              role="tab"
              aria-selected={tab === item.id}
              className={tab === item.id ? 'tab active' : 'tab'}
              onClick={() => setTab(item.id)}
            >
              {item.label}
            </button>
          ))}
        </div>
        {content}
      </section>
    </main>
  );
}

export default StudentDetailPage;
The tab is just a piece of state on the page. Views don't share their own state, so each starts fresh when you switch back to it. The two new views get the full periods answer (they use current and the list), while HistoryGrid still gets just the list, as before.

Sample data, then test
One student with only a few scores won't show much, so let's give the newest student a proper history. It uses the same helpers as before, so paste the lines into one terminal tab:

pid() { curl -s http://localhost:3000/api/parameters | node -e "let s='';process.stdin.on('data',d=>s+=d).on('end',()=>console.log(JSON.parse(s).parameters.find(p=>p.name==='$1').id))"; }
sid() { curl -s "http://localhost:3000/api/students?pageSize=5" | node -e "let s='';process.stdin.on('data',d=>s+=d).on('end',()=>console.log(JSON.parse(s).students[$1].id))"; }
e() { echo "{\"studentId\":\"$1\",\"parameterId\":\"$2\",\"value\":$3}"; }
save() { curl -s -o /dev/null -w "HTTP %{http_code}\n" -X PUT http://localhost:3000/api/evaluations/bulk -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d "$1"; }
SPEED=$(pid Speed); RHYTHM=$(pid Rhythm); BALANCE=$(pid Balance); S1=$(sid 0)
CUR=$(curl -s http://localhost:3000/api/periods | node -e "let s='';process.stdin.on('data',d=>s+=d).on('end',()=>console.log(JSON.parse(s).current))")
echo "current period: $CUR"
for n in $(seq 3 $CUR); do [ $n -eq 5 ] && continue; save "{\"periodNumber\":$n,\"entries\":[$(e $S1 $SPEED $((n % 5 + 4)))]}"; done
for n in $(seq 3 9); do save "{\"periodNumber\":$n,\"entries\":[$(e $S1 $RHYTHM $((n % 4 + 3)))]}"; done
for n in $(seq 6 $CUR); do save "{\"periodNumber\":$n,\"entries\":[$(e $S1 $BALANCE $((n % 3 + 5)))]}"; done
This gives that student Speed in every period except 5 (a deliberate gap), Rhythm in periods 3 to 9, and Balance from period 6. Lines saying HTTP 400 are only for periods that haven't started yet, if your current period is low. Start the server and client, open that student from the Students list, and check:

Three tabs appear: Overview, By period, By parameter.
Overview shows the history you just created, with "—" at Period 5 for Speed, and "n/a" for Balance before Period 5 and for Rhythm from Period 10.
By period opens on the latest scored period, with one row per parameter, a bar under "Within range", and a ▲ or ▼ change. Use Previous to go back. In Period 5, Speed shows "—" (in use, not measured). In Period 4, only Speed and Rhythm are listed.
By parameter → Speed: a line chart with a visible break at P5, and a summary line with the latest, the average, and the change since the first score. The table below shows "—" for Period 5.
By parameter → Rhythm is labelled "(not in use now)", and its history still shows (periods 3 to 9).
By parameter → Balance: the chart starts at its first score (P6).
Open a student with no scores. By period shows only "—" rows for the current period, and By parameter says "No scores yet."
On the Evaluate page, change one of this student's scores and save. Return to the student page (back button), and the numbers have updated.

I put login first because the edit locks need roles (for the admin override) and need to know who saved each score.

Piece 20: login and roles, server side
Right now anyone who can reach the server can read and change everything. After this piece, the server refuses everything unless you're signed in, and each role can only do what it's allowed to.

Authentication asks who you are (username and password).
Authorization asks what you're allowed to do (your role).
The roles

What	Viewer	Staff	Admin
See students, parameters, scores, and views	✓	✓	✓
Add and rename students, save scores		✓	✓
Delete students			✓
Parameters, parameter changes, start date			✓
Manage users (piece 22)			✓
Each row is a rule in the code, so it's easy to change if you want different permissions.

How it works

Passwords are never stored. Only a hash is saved, a one-way scramble made with argon2, which is deliberately slow so guessing is expensive.
A session is how the server remembers you after login. It keeps a record in the database and gives your browser a random ID in a cookie. The cookie is httpOnly (page scripts can't read it) and SameSite=Lax (browsers don't send it on requests started by other sites).
New accounts get a temporary password, shown once. On first login you must choose your own.
Default deny: every route says which role may use it, and anything not listed is refused.
Login attempts are limited: 10 failures in 15 minutes from one address, and then it blocks for a while.
A warning: once this piece is done, the web app will show "Please sign in." errors until piece 21 adds the login page. That's expected.

cd /data/projects/student-panel/server
A. Install the libraries

npm install argon2 express-session connect-pg-simple express-rate-limit
argon2 normally arrives ready-made. If npm tries to compile it and fails, run sudo apt install -y build-essential python3 and install again.

B. A secret for the sessions

The server signs its cookies with a secret. Add one to .env:

printf '\nSESSION_SECRET=%s\n' "$(openssl rand -hex 32)" >> .env
grep -c SESSION_SECRET .env
git check-ignore .env
The second command should print 1, and the third should print .env. Never share this line or commit it. If the secret changes later, everyone is signed out.

C. The tables

Create src/db/migrations/005_create_users_and_sessions.sql:

sql
CREATE TABLE users (
  id                   uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  username             text NOT NULL,
  full_name            text NOT NULL,
  password_hash        text NOT NULL,
  role                 text NOT NULL,
  is_active            boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true,
  must_change_password boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true,
  last_login_at        timestamptz,
  created_at           timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  updated_at           timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  CONSTRAINT users_role_valid CHECK (role IN ('viewer', 'staff', 'admin')),
  CONSTRAINT users_username_format CHECK (username ~ '^[a-z0-9._-]{3,32}$'),
  CONSTRAINT users_full_name_length
    CHECK (char_length(btrim(full_name)) BETWEEN 1 AND 100)
);

CREATE UNIQUE INDEX users_username_unique ON users (lower(username));

CREATE TABLE user_sessions (
  sid    varchar PRIMARY KEY,
  sess   json NOT NULL,
  expire timestamp(6) NOT NULL
);

CREATE INDEX user_sessions_expire_idx ON user_sessions (expire);
npm run migrate
You should see Applied 005_create_users_and_sessions.sql.

D. The building blocks

Create src/lib/roles.js:

js
export const ROLES = ['viewer', 'staff', 'admin'];

const LEVEL = { viewer: 1, staff: 2, admin: 3 };

export function isRole(value) {
  return typeof value === 'string' && Object.hasOwn(LEVEL, value);
}

export function hasAtLeast(role, minRole) {
  if (!isRole(role) || !isRole(minRole)) return false;
  return LEVEL[role] >= LEVEL[minRole];
}

export function minRoleFor(policy, method) {
  const name = method === 'HEAD' ? 'GET' : method;
  return Object.hasOwn(policy, name) ? policy[name] : null;
}
Roles have an order (viewer < staff < admin), so "staff or better" is one comparison. Anything unknown is refused, and minRoleFor returns null (meaning "not allowed") for a method the policy doesn't mention.

Create src/lib/passwords.js:

js
import { randomInt } from 'node:crypto';
import argon2 from 'argon2';

export const MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH = 10;
export const MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH = 128;

const ALPHABET =
  '23456789ABCDEFGHJKLMNPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijkmnpqrstuvwxyz';

export function hashPassword(password) {
  return argon2.hash(password);
}

export async function verifyPassword(hash, password) {
  try {
    return await argon2.verify(hash, password);
  } catch {
    return false;
  }
}

export function generatePassword(length = 16) {
  let password = '';
  for (let i = 0; i < length; i++) {
    password += ALPHABET[randomInt(ALPHABET.length)];
  }
  return password;
}

export function validateNewPassword(password, { username } = {}) {
  if (typeof password !== 'string') return 'A new password is required.';
  if (password.length < MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH) {
    return `The password must be at least ${MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH} characters.`;
  }
  if (password.length > MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH) {
    return `The password must be at most ${MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH} characters.`;
  }
  if (username && password.toLowerCase() === username.toLowerCase()) {
    return 'The password cannot be the same as the username.';
  }
  return null;
}
The rule is deliberately simple: length matters much more than symbols, so a passphrase like correct-horse-battery is welcome. Temporary passwords use an alphabet without look-alike characters, made with the same secure random source as student codes.

Create src/validation/user.js:

js
const USERNAME_PATTERN = /^[a-z0-9._-]{3,32}$/;

export function parseUsername(input) {
  if (typeof input !== 'string') return { error: 'Username is required.' };
  const value = input.trim().toLowerCase();
  if (!USERNAME_PATTERN.test(value)) {
    return {
      error:
        'Username must be 3 to 32 characters: letters, digits, dots, dashes or underscores.',
    };
  }
  return { value };
}
Create src/lib/users.js:

js
import pool from '../db/pool.js';
import { hashPassword } from './passwords.js';

export const USER_COLUMNS = `
  id,
  username,
  full_name            AS "fullName",
  role,
  is_active            AS "isActive",
  must_change_password AS "mustChangePassword",
  last_login_at        AS "lastLoginAt",
  created_at           AS "createdAt"
`;

export async function createUser({
  username,
  fullName,
  role,
  password,
  mustChangePassword = true,
}) {
  const result = await pool.query(
    `INSERT INTO users (username, full_name, role, password_hash, must_change_password)
     VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5)
     RETURNING ${USER_COLUMNS}`,
    [username, fullName, role, await hashPassword(password), mustChangePassword]
  );
  return result.rows[0];
}
USER_COLUMNS deliberately leaves out the password hash, so it can never leak in an answer by accident.

E. Tests

Create test/auth.test.js:

js
import { test } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { ROLES, hasAtLeast, isRole, minRoleFor } from '../src/lib/roles.js';
import {
  generatePassword,
  hashPassword,
  validateNewPassword,
  verifyPassword,
} from '../src/lib/passwords.js';
import { parseUsername } from '../src/validation/user.js';

test('roles are ordered viewer < staff < admin', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(ROLES, ['viewer', 'staff', 'admin']);
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast('admin', 'staff'), true);
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast('staff', 'staff'), true);
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast('viewer', 'staff'), false);
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast('staff', 'admin'), false);
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast('viewer', 'viewer'), true);
});

test('unknown roles are never allowed', () => {
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast('hacker', 'viewer'), false);
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast(undefined, 'viewer'), false);
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast('admin', 'nonsense'), false);
  assert.equal(isRole('admin'), true);
  assert.equal(isRole('toString'), false);
  assert.equal(isRole(5), false);
});

test('a policy maps methods to minimum roles and denies the rest', () => {
  const policy = { GET: 'viewer', POST: 'staff' };
  assert.equal(minRoleFor(policy, 'GET'), 'viewer');
  assert.equal(minRoleFor(policy, 'HEAD'), 'viewer');
  assert.equal(minRoleFor(policy, 'POST'), 'staff');
  assert.equal(minRoleFor(policy, 'DELETE'), null);
  assert.equal(minRoleFor(policy, 'OPTIONS'), null);
  assert.equal(minRoleFor(policy, 'toString'), null);
});

test('passwords are hashed and verified', async () => {
  const hash = await hashPassword('correct-horse-battery');
  assert.notEqual(hash, 'correct-horse-battery');
  assert.ok(hash.startsWith('$argon2'));
  assert.equal(await verifyPassword(hash, 'correct-horse-battery'), true);
  assert.equal(await verifyPassword(hash, 'wrong-horse-battery'), false);
  assert.equal(await verifyPassword('not a real hash', 'anything'), false);
});

test('new passwords are checked', () => {
  assert.equal(validateNewPassword('long-enough-password'), null);
  assert.ok(validateNewPassword('short'));
  assert.ok(validateNewPassword(undefined));
  assert.ok(validateNewPassword('x'.repeat(129)));
  assert.ok(validateNewPassword('Ada.Lovelace', { username: 'ada.lovelace' }));
});

test('generated passwords use the safe alphabet and differ each time', () => {
  const a = generatePassword();
  const b = generatePassword();
  assert.equal(a.length, 16);
  assert.match(a, /^[2-9A-HJ-NP-Za-km-np-z]+$/);
  assert.notEqual(a, b);
  assert.equal(generatePassword(24).length, 24);
});

test('usernames are normalised and validated', () => {
  assert.deepEqual(parseUsername('  Ada.Lovelace '), { value: 'ada.lovelace' });
  assert.ok(parseUsername('ab').error);
  assert.ok(parseUsername('has space').error);
  assert.ok(parseUsername('x'.repeat(33)).error);
  assert.ok(parseUsername(undefined).error);
});
npm test
You should see 7 new green tests, for a total of pass 36 and fail 0 (29 + 7). If any fail, send me the output before continuing.

F. The middleware

Create src/middleware/auth.js:

js
import pool from '../db/pool.js';
import { hasAtLeast, minRoleFor } from '../lib/roles.js';
import { USER_COLUMNS } from '../lib/users.js';

export async function attachUser(req, res, next) {
  const userId = req.session?.userId;
  if (!userId) return next();

  const result = await pool.query(
    `SELECT ${USER_COLUMNS} FROM users WHERE id = $1 AND is_active`,
    [userId]
  );
  if (result.rowCount === 0) {
    await new Promise((resolve) => req.session.destroy(resolve));
    return next();
  }
  req.user = result.rows[0];
  next();
}

export function requireLogin(req, res, next) {
  if (!req.user) {
    return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Please sign in.' });
  }
  next();
}

export function authorize(policy) {
  return (req, res, next) => {
    if (!req.user) {
      return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Please sign in.' });
    }
    if (req.user.mustChangePassword) {
      return res.status(403).json({
        error: 'You must change your password first.',
        code: 'PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED',
      });
    }
    const minRole = minRoleFor(policy, req.method);
    if (minRole === null || !hasAtLeast(req.user.role, minRole)) {
      return res
        .status(403)
        .json({ error: 'You do not have permission to do that.' });
    }
    next();
  };
}
attachUser runs on every request. It looks up the person behind the session cookie and puts them on req.user. It reads the database each time, so if you deactivate someone or change their role, it takes effect on their next click, not at the end of their session.
authorize(policy) is the gatekeeper. It refuses if nobody is signed in (401), if the person still has a temporary password (403 with a code the React app will recognize), or if their role is too low for the request's method (403).
G. The login routes

Create src/routes/auth.js:

js
import { Router } from 'express';
import { rateLimit } from 'express-rate-limit';
import pool from '../db/pool.js';
import {
  MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH,
  hashPassword,
  validateNewPassword,
  verifyPassword,
} from '../lib/passwords.js';
import { USER_COLUMNS } from '../lib/users.js';
import { requireLogin } from '../middleware/auth.js';

const router = Router();

let dummyHash;
async function getDummyHash() {
  dummyHash ??= await hashPassword('a password that nobody uses');
  return dummyHash;
}

function viaCallback(run) {
  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    run((err) => (err ? reject(err) : resolve()));
  });
}

const loginLimiter = rateLimit({
  windowMs: 15 * 60 * 1000,
  limit: 10,
  skipSuccessfulRequests: true,
  standardHeaders: true,
  legacyHeaders: false,
  message: {
    error: 'Too many failed attempts. Please wait 15 minutes and try again.',
  },
});

router.post('/login', loginLimiter, async (req, res) => {
  const username =
    typeof req.body?.username === 'string'
      ? req.body.username.trim().toLowerCase()
      : '';
  const password =
    typeof req.body?.password === 'string' ? req.body.password : '';

  if (!username || !password || password.length > MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Enter your username and password.' });
  }

  const result = await pool.query(
    `SELECT ${USER_COLUMNS}, password_hash AS "passwordHash"
     FROM users WHERE lower(username) = $1`,
    [username]
  );
  const found = result.rows[0];

  const hash = found ? found.passwordHash : await getDummyHash();
  const passwordOk = await verifyPassword(hash, password);
  if (!found || !found.isActive || !passwordOk) {
    return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Wrong username or password.' });
  }

  await viaCallback((done) => req.session.regenerate(done));
  req.session.userId = found.id;
  await viaCallback((done) => req.session.save(done));

  await pool.query('UPDATE users SET last_login_at = now() WHERE id = $1', [
    found.id,
  ]);

  const { passwordHash, ...user } = found;
  res.json({ user });
});

router.post('/logout', async (req, res) => {
  await viaCallback((done) => req.session.destroy(done));
  res.clearCookie('sid');
  res.status(204).end();
});

router.get('/me', (req, res) => {
  if (!req.user) {
    return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Not signed in.' });
  }
  res.json({ user: req.user });
});

router.post('/change-password', requireLogin, async (req, res) => {
  const { currentPassword, newPassword } = req.body ?? {};

  if (
    typeof currentPassword !== 'string' ||
    currentPassword === '' ||
    currentPassword.length > MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH
  ) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Enter your current password.' });
  }
  const problem = validateNewPassword(newPassword, {
    username: req.user.username,
  });
  if (problem) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: problem });
  }
  if (newPassword === currentPassword) {
    return res.status(400).json({
      error: 'The new password must be different from the current one.',
    });
  }

  const found = await pool.query(
    'SELECT password_hash AS "passwordHash" FROM users WHERE id = $1',
    [req.user.id]
  );
  if (!(await verifyPassword(found.rows[0].passwordHash, currentPassword))) {
    return res
      .status(400)
      .json({ error: 'The current password is incorrect.' });
  }

  await pool.query(
    `UPDATE users
     SET password_hash = $1, must_change_password = false, updated_at = now()
     WHERE id = $2`,
    [await hashPassword(newPassword), req.user.id]
  );
  await pool.query(
    `DELETE FROM user_sessions WHERE (sess->>'userId') = $1 AND sid <> $2`,
    [req.user.id, req.sessionID]
  );

  res.json({ user: { ...req.user, mustChangePassword: false } });
});

export default router;
Worth knowing:

Same message for "no such user" and "wrong password." An attacker learns nothing about which usernames exist. The getDummyHash() step makes an unknown user take about as long as a wrong password, so timing doesn't give it away either.
regenerate on login issues a fresh session ID, which blocks "session fixation" (someone planting a session ID in your browser before you log in).
Changing a password signs out that account's other sessions. If someone had stolen a session, the new password cuts them off.
The rate limiter counts only failures (skipSuccessfulRequests).
H. Protect everything

Replace all of src/index.js with:

js
import connectPgSimple from 'connect-pg-simple';
import express from 'express';
import session from 'express-session';
import pool from './db/pool.js';
import { attachUser, authorize, requireLogin } from './middleware/auth.js';
import authRouter from './routes/auth.js';
import evaluationsRouter from './routes/evaluations.js';
import parametersRouter from './routes/parameters.js';
import periodsRouter from './routes/periods.js';
import studentsRouter from './routes/students.js';

const secret = process.env.SESSION_SECRET;
if (!secret || secret.length < 32) {
  console.error(
    'SESSION_SECRET is missing or too short. Add a line like ' +
      'SESSION_SECRET=<64 random characters> to server/.env, then restart.'
  );
  process.exit(1);
}

const PgSession = connectPgSimple(session);
const app = express();
app.disable('x-powered-by');
app.use(express.json());

app.get('/api/health', (req, res) => {
  res.json({ ok: true });
});

app.use(
  session({
    store: new PgSession({ pool, tableName: 'user_sessions' }),
    name: 'sid',
    secret,
    resave: false,
    saveUninitialized: false,
    rolling: true,
    cookie: {
      httpOnly: true,
      sameSite: 'lax',
      secure: 'auto',
      maxAge: 8 * 60 * 60 * 1000,
    },
  })
);
app.use('/api', attachUser);

app.use('/api/auth', authRouter);
app.use('/api', requireLogin);

app.use(
  '/api/students',
  authorize({ GET: 'viewer', POST: 'staff', PATCH: 'staff', DELETE: 'admin' }),
  studentsRouter
);
app.use('/api/parameters', authorize({ GET: 'viewer', POST: 'admin', PATCH: 'admin' }));
app.use(
  '/api/parameter-changes',
  authorize({ GET: 'viewer', POST: 'admin', DELETE: 'admin' })
);
app.use('/api/periods', authorize({ GET: 'viewer' }));
app.use('/api/settings', authorize({ GET: 'viewer', PUT: 'admin' }));
app.use('/api/evaluations', authorize({ GET: 'viewer', PUT: 'staff' }));

app.use('/api', periodsRouter);
app.use('/api', parametersRouter);
app.use('/api', evaluationsRouter);

app.use('/api', (req, res) => {
  res.status(404).json({ error: 'Not found.' });
});

app.use((err, req, res, next) => {
  const status = err.status || 500;
  if (status >= 500) console.error(err);
  res.status(status).json({
    error: status >= 500 ? 'Something went wrong on the server.' : err.message,
  });
});

const port = process.env.PORT || 3000;
app.listen(port, () => {
  console.log(`Server running at http://localhost:${port}`);
});
What changed:

The test route /api/db-check is gone.
The server refuses to start without a proper SESSION_SECRET.
Order matters. The health check and login routes are public. Then requireLogin covers everything else under /api, even a route we forget to list. After that, each area gets its own role policy: authorize(...) is a gate for that address prefix, then the real router handles the request.
Unknown API addresses now answer with JSON instead of an HTML error page.
I. Create the first admin

Create src/scripts/createUser.js:

js
import pool from '../db/pool.js';
import { ROLES, isRole } from '../lib/roles.js';
import { generatePassword } from '../lib/passwords.js';
import { createUser } from '../lib/users.js';
import { parseUsername } from '../validation/user.js';

function fail(message) {
  console.error(message);
  process.exit(1);
}

const [rawUsername, rawFullName, role = 'admin'] = process.argv.slice(2);

const username = parseUsername(rawUsername);
if (username.error) fail(username.error);

const fullName = (rawFullName ?? '').trim();
if (fullName.length < 1 || fullName.length > 100) {
  fail('Give a full name of 1 to 100 characters, in quotes.');
}
if (!isRole(role)) fail(`Role must be one of: ${ROLES.join(', ')}.`);

const password = generatePassword();
try {
  await createUser({
    username: username.value,
    fullName,
    role,
    password,
    mustChangePassword: true,
  });
} catch (err) {
  if (err.code === '23505') fail('That username already exists.');
  throw err;
}

console.log(`Created ${role} "${username.value}".`);
console.log(`Temporary password (shown once): ${password}`);
console.log('The user must choose a new password at first login.');
await pool.end();
Then add the script and run it. Pick your own username and name:

npm pkg set scripts.create-user="node --env-file=.env src/scripts/createUser.js"
npm run create-user -- admin "Site Admin" admin
The -- separates npm's own options from the ones passed to the script. It prints a temporary password. Copy it somewhere safe, since it can't be shown again. If you lose it, delete the row (DELETE FROM users WHERE username = 'admin';) and create the account again.

Now restart the server (.env changed, so Ctrl+C, then npm run dev). You should see the "Server running" line. If it complains about SESSION_SECRET, re-check step B.

J. Try it

These use a small helper and "cookie jars" (files where curl keeps its login cookie, like a browser). Run them in one terminal tab:

api() { curl -s -w "\nHTTP %{http_code}\n" -H "Content-Type: application/json" "$@"; }
Not signed in:

api http://localhost:3000/api/students
api -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/auth/login -d '{"username":"admin","password":"wrong-password"}'
api http://localhost:3000/api/health
Expected: 401 "Please sign in." Then 401 "Wrong username or password." Then 200 with {"ok":true}.

First login with the temporary password. Put yours in place of the text:

TEMP='paste-the-temporary-password-here'
api -c /tmp/admin.jar -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/auth/login -d "{\"username\":\"admin\",\"password\":\"$TEMP\"}"
api -b /tmp/admin.jar http://localhost:3000/api/auth/me
api -b /tmp/admin.jar http://localhost:3000/api/students
Expected: 200 with "mustChangePassword":true, 200 for me, then 403 with PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED.

Change the password:

api -b /tmp/admin.jar -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/auth/change-password -d "{\"currentPassword\":\"$TEMP\",\"newPassword\":\"short\"}"
api -b /tmp/admin.jar -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/auth/change-password -d '{"currentPassword":"not-the-right-one","newPassword":"a-much-longer-password"}'
api -b /tmp/admin.jar -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/auth/change-password -d "{\"currentPassword\":\"$TEMP\",\"newPassword\":\"correct-horse-battery\"}"
api -b /tmp/admin.jar http://localhost:3000/api/students
Expected: 400 (too short), 400 (current password incorrect), 200 with "mustChangePassword":false, and then 200 with your students. Your admin password is now correct-horse-battery, so use your own passphrase if you prefer, and remember it.

The hash is not the password:

psql -h localhost -U stu_manager -d stu_database -c "SELECT username, role, left(password_hash, 14) AS hash_start FROM users;"
You should see something starting with $argon2.

Roles. Create a staff account and a viewer account. Each command prints a temporary password. Then skip the forced password change for these two test accounts, which is a shortcut for testing only:

npm run create-user -- staff1 "Sam Staff" staff
npm run create-user -- viewer1 "Vera Viewer" viewer
psql -h localhost -U stu_manager -d stu_database -c "UPDATE users SET must_change_password = false WHERE username IN ('staff1','viewer1');"
ST='paste-the-staff-temporary-password'
VW='paste-the-viewer-temporary-password'
api -c /tmp/staff.jar -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/auth/login -d "{\"username\":\"staff1\",\"password\":\"$ST\"}"
api -c /tmp/viewer.jar -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/auth/login -d "{\"username\":\"viewer1\",\"password\":\"$VW\"}"
Then try what each role can and can't do:

api -b /tmp/viewer.jar http://localhost:3000/api/students
api -b /tmp/viewer.jar -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/students -d '{"fullName":"Blocked Student"}'
api -b /tmp/viewer.jar -X PUT http://localhost:3000/api/settings/period-anchor -d '{"anchorDate":"2026-09-19"}'
api -b /tmp/staff.jar -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/students -d '{"fullName":"Staff Made Student"}'
api -b /tmp/staff.jar -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/parameters -d '{"name":"Nope","startPeriod":1}'
api -b /tmp/staff.jar -X DELETE http://localhost:3000/api/students/00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000
api -b /tmp/admin.jar -X DELETE http://localhost:3000/api/students/00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000
Expected results:

Viewer reads the list: 200
Viewer adds a student: 403 "You do not have permission to do that."
Viewer changes the start date: 403
Staff adds a student: 201
Staff adds a parameter: 403
Staff deletes: 403
Admin deletes that (non-existent) student: 404 "Student not found." This proves the admin got past the gate.
Logout:

api -b /tmp/admin.jar -c /tmp/admin.jar -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/auth/logout
api -b /tmp/admin.jar http://localhost:3000/api/auth/me
Expected: 204, then 401.

Login attempts are limited. Run this last, because it blocks logins from this computer for 15 minutes:

for i in $(seq 1 12); do curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code} " -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/auth/login -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{"username":"admin","password":"wrong-password"}'; done; echo
You should see about nine or ten 401s followed by 429s. The counter lives in the server's memory, so stopping and restarting the server resets it.

If something goes wrong

The server exits with a SESSION_SECRET message: step B didn't apply. Check grep SESSION_SECRET .env shows a long value, then restart.
Cannot find package 'argon2' (or another package): the install in step A didn't finish, so run it again.
Login always says "Wrong username or password": check the temporary password was copied exactly. Or the account was deleted and re-created, in which case use the new password.
Every request says "Please sign in" even after login: you probably forgot -b /tmp/admin.jar on that command.
relation "users" does not exist: run npm run migrate.
A test fails: send me the failing output.

cd /data/projects/student-panel/client
A. Talking to the login routes

Create src/api/auth.js:

js
import { request } from './http.js';

export async function fetchMe() {
  try {
    const data = await request('/api/auth/me');
    return data.user;
  } catch (error) {
    if (error.status === 401) return null;
    throw error;
  }
}

export async function login({ username, password }) {
  const data = await request('/api/auth/login', {
    method: 'POST',
    body: JSON.stringify({ username, password }),
  });
  return data.user;
}

export function logout() {
  return request('/api/auth/logout', { method: 'POST' });
}

export async function changePassword({ currentPassword, newPassword }) {
  const data = await request('/api/auth/change-password', {
    method: 'POST',
    body: JSON.stringify({ currentPassword, newPassword }),
  });
  return data.user;
}
fetchMe treats "not signed in" (401) as a normal answer, null, instead of an error. Other failures, like a stopped server, still count as errors. The browser sends the session cookie automatically, since requests go to the same address.

Create src/auth/roles.js:

js
const LEVEL = { viewer: 1, staff: 2, admin: 3 };

export function hasAtLeast(role, minRole) {
  return (
    Object.hasOwn(LEVEL, role) &&
    Object.hasOwn(LEVEL, minRole) &&
    LEVEL[role] >= LEVEL[minRole]
  );
}
It's the same ordering rule as the server's, so the screens can hide what the server would refuse. Create test/roles.test.js:

js
import { test } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { hasAtLeast } from '../src/auth/roles.js';

test('roles are ordered and unknown roles never pass', () => {
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast('admin', 'staff'), true);
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast('staff', 'staff'), true);
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast('viewer', 'staff'), false);
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast('viewer', 'viewer'), true);
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast('hacker', 'viewer'), false);
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast(undefined, 'viewer'), false);
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast('admin', 'nonsense'), false);
  assert.equal(hasAtLeast('toString', 'viewer'), false);
});
npm test
You should see pass 20 and fail 0 (19 + 1).

B. "Who is signed in" for the whole app

Create src/auth/AuthContext.jsx:

jsx
import { createContext, useContext } from 'react';
import { useMutation, useQuery, useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { changePassword, fetchMe, login, logout } from '../api/auth.js';
import { hasAtLeast } from './roles.js';

const AuthContext = createContext(null);

export function AuthProvider({ children }) {
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();

  const meQuery = useQuery({
    queryKey: ['me'],
    queryFn: fetchMe,
    retry: false,
  });
  const user = meQuery.data ?? null;

  function forgetEverythingExceptMe() {
    queryClient.removeQueries({
      predicate: (query) => query.queryKey[0] !== 'me',
    });
  }

  const loginMutation = useMutation({
    mutationFn: login,
    onSuccess: (signedInUser) => {
      forgetEverythingExceptMe();
      queryClient.setQueryData(['me'], signedInUser);
    },
  });

  const logoutMutation = useMutation({
    mutationFn: logout,
    onSettled: () => {
      forgetEverythingExceptMe();
      queryClient.setQueryData(['me'], null);
    },
  });

  const changePasswordMutation = useMutation({
    mutationFn: changePassword,
    onSuccess: (updatedUser) => {
      queryClient.setQueryData(['me'], updatedUser);
    },
  });

  const value = {
    user,
    isLoading: meQuery.isPending,
    loadError: meQuery.isError ? meQuery.error : null,
    can: (minRole) => user !== null && hasAtLeast(user.role, minRole),
    login: loginMutation,
    logout: logoutMutation,
    changePassword: changePasswordMutation,
  };

  return <AuthContext.Provider value={value}>{children}</AuthContext.Provider>;
}

export function useAuth() {
  const context = useContext(AuthContext);
  if (context === null) {
    throw new Error('useAuth must be used inside an AuthProvider.');
  }
  return context;
}
A context is a way to share something with every component without passing it down by hand. AuthProvider holds the answer to "who am I?" (user), and any component can ask for it with useAuth().
can('staff') answers "is the signed-in person at least staff?", which is what we use to show or hide buttons.
The user lives in TanStack Query's cache under the key ['me']. Signing in or out just puts a new value there.
On sign-in and sign-out we throw away every other cached answer (forgetEverythingExceptMe). Otherwise the next person on the same computer could briefly see the previous person's student data.
C. Router guard and the pages

Create src/components/RequireAuth.jsx:

jsx
import { Navigate, Outlet, useLocation } from 'react-router';
import { useAuth } from '../auth/AuthContext.jsx';

function RequireAuth() {
  const { user, isLoading, loadError } = useAuth();
  const location = useLocation();

  if (isLoading) {
    return (
      <main>
        <p>Loading...</p>
      </main>
    );
  }
  if (loadError) {
    return (
      <main>
        <p className="error">{loadError.message}</p>
      </main>
    );
  }
  if (!user) {
    return <Navigate to="/login" replace state={{ from: location }} />;
  }
  if (user.mustChangePassword && location.pathname !== '/change-password') {
    return <Navigate to="/change-password" replace />;
  }
  return <Outlet />;
}

export default RequireAuth;
It wraps every page that needs a signed-in user. If nobody is signed in, it sends you to the login page and remembers where you were headed (state.from), so you land back there after signing in. If you still have a temporary password, it sends you to the change-password page instead.

Create src/pages/LoginPage.jsx:

jsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import { Navigate, useLocation } from 'react-router';
import { useAuth } from '../auth/AuthContext.jsx';

function LoginPage() {
  const { user, isLoading, login } = useAuth();
  const location = useLocation();
  const [username, setUsername] = useState('');
  const [password, setPassword] = useState('');

  if (isLoading) {
    return (
      <main className="narrow">
        <p>Loading...</p>
      </main>
    );
  }
  if (user) {
    const from = location.state?.from?.pathname ?? '/students';
    return <Navigate to={from} replace />;
  }

  function handleSubmit(event) {
    event.preventDefault();
    login.mutate({ username, password });
  }

  return (
    <main className="narrow">
      <h1>Sign in</h1>
      <form onSubmit={handleSubmit} className="edit-form">
        <label>
          Username
          <input
            value={username}
            onChange={(event) => setUsername(event.target.value)}
            autoComplete="username"
            autoFocus
          />
        </label>
        <label>
          Password
          <input
            type="password"
            value={password}
            onChange={(event) => setPassword(event.target.value)}
            autoComplete="current-password"
          />
        </label>
        <button type="submit" disabled={login.isPending}>
          {login.isPending ? 'Signing in...' : 'Sign in'}
        </button>
        {login.isError && <p className="error">{login.error.message}</p>}
      </form>
    </main>
  );
}

export default LoginPage;
Create src/pages/ChangePasswordPage.jsx:

jsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import { useNavigate } from 'react-router';
import { useAuth } from '../auth/AuthContext.jsx';

const MIN_LENGTH = 10;

function ChangePasswordPage() {
  const { user, changePassword } = useAuth();
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const [currentPassword, setCurrentPassword] = useState('');
  const [newPassword, setNewPassword] = useState('');
  const [repeat, setRepeat] = useState('');
  const [problem, setProblem] = useState(null);

  function handleSubmit(event) {
    event.preventDefault();

    if (newPassword.length < MIN_LENGTH) {
      changePassword.reset();
      setProblem(`The new password must be at least ${MIN_LENGTH} characters.`);
      return;
    }
    if (newPassword !== repeat) {
      changePassword.reset();
      setProblem('The two new passwords do not match.');
      return;
    }

    setProblem(null);
    const wasForced = user.mustChangePassword;
    changePassword.mutate(
      { currentPassword, newPassword },
      {
        onSuccess: () => {
          setCurrentPassword('');
          setNewPassword('');
          setRepeat('');
          if (wasForced) navigate('/students', { replace: true });
        },
      }
    );
  }

  return (
    <main className="narrow">
      <h1>Change password</h1>
      {user.mustChangePassword && (
        <p>You are using a temporary password. Choose a new one to continue.</p>
      )}
      <form onSubmit={handleSubmit} className="edit-form">
        <label>
          Current password
          <input
            type="password"
            value={currentPassword}
            onChange={(event) => setCurrentPassword(event.target.value)}
            autoComplete="current-password"
          />
        </label>
        <label>
          New password
          <input
            type="password"
            value={newPassword}
            onChange={(event) => setNewPassword(event.target.value)}
            autoComplete="new-password"
          />
        </label>
        <label>
          Repeat new password
          <input
            type="password"
            value={repeat}
            onChange={(event) => setRepeat(event.target.value)}
            autoComplete="new-password"
          />
        </label>
        <button type="submit" disabled={changePassword.isPending}>
          {changePassword.isPending ? 'Saving...' : 'Change password'}
        </button>
        {problem && <p className="error">{problem}</p>}
        {changePassword.isError && (
          <p className="error">{changePassword.error.message}</p>
        )}
        {changePassword.isSuccess && !user.mustChangePassword && (
          <p className="success">
            Password changed. Your other sessions were signed out.
          </p>
        )}
      </form>
      <p className="hint">
        Use at least {MIN_LENGTH} characters. Several random words work well.
      </p>
    </main>
  );
}

export default ChangePasswordPage;
D. Wire it together

Replace all of src/main.jsx with:

jsx
import { StrictMode } from 'react';
import { createRoot } from 'react-dom/client';
import { BrowserRouter } from 'react-router';
import {
  MutationCache,
  QueryCache,
  QueryClient,
  QueryClientProvider,
} from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { AuthProvider } from './auth/AuthContext.jsx';
import './index.css';
import App from './App.jsx';

function signOutOn401(error) {
  if (error?.status === 401) {
    queryClient.setQueryData(['me'], null);
  }
}

const queryClient = new QueryClient({
  queryCache: new QueryCache({ onError: signOutOn401 }),
  mutationCache: new MutationCache({ onError: signOutOn401 }),
  defaultOptions: {
    queries: {
      retry: (failureCount, error) =>
        error?.status === undefined && failureCount < 1,
    },
  },
});

createRoot(document.getElementById('root')).render(
  <StrictMode>
    <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
      <BrowserRouter>
        <AuthProvider>
          <App />
        </AuthProvider>
      </BrowserRouter>
    </QueryClientProvider>
  </StrictMode>
);
signOutOn401 handles a session that ends in the middle of your work (it expired, or an admin deactivated you). Any request answered 401 marks you as signed out, and the guard sends you to the login page.
retry now only retries network failures. There's no point retrying an answer like "not allowed."
Replace all of src/App.jsx with:

jsx
import { Navigate, Route, Routes } from 'react-router';
import Layout from './components/Layout.jsx';
import RequireAuth from './components/RequireAuth.jsx';
import ChangePasswordPage from './pages/ChangePasswordPage.jsx';
import EvaluatePage from './pages/EvaluatePage.jsx';
import LoginPage from './pages/LoginPage.jsx';
import NotFoundPage from './pages/NotFoundPage.jsx';
import ParametersPage from './pages/ParametersPage.jsx';
import SettingsPage from './pages/SettingsPage.jsx';
import StudentDetailPage from './pages/StudentDetailPage.jsx';
import StudentsPage from './pages/StudentsPage.jsx';

function App() {
  return (
    <Routes>
      <Route path="/login" element={<LoginPage />} />
      <Route element={<RequireAuth />}>
        <Route element={<Layout />}>
          <Route path="/" element={<Navigate to="/students" replace />} />
          <Route path="/students" element={<StudentsPage />} />
          <Route path="/students/:id" element={<StudentDetailPage />} />
          <Route path="/evaluate" element={<EvaluatePage />} />
          <Route path="/parameters" element={<ParametersPage />} />
          <Route path="/settings" element={<SettingsPage />} />
          <Route path="/change-password" element={<ChangePasswordPage />} />
          <Route path="*" element={<NotFoundPage />} />
        </Route>
      </Route>
    </Routes>
  );
}

export default App;
The login page is public. Every other page sits inside RequireAuth, and then inside Layout.

Replace all of src/components/Layout.jsx with:

jsx
import { NavLink, Outlet } from 'react-router';
import { useAuth } from '../auth/AuthContext.jsx';

function Layout() {
  const { user, can, logout } = useAuth();

  return (
    <>
      <header className="topbar">
        <nav>
          {!user.mustChangePassword && (
            <>
              <NavLink to="/students">Students</NavLink>
              {can('staff') && <NavLink to="/evaluate">Evaluate</NavLink>}
              <NavLink to="/parameters">Parameters</NavLink>
              <NavLink to="/settings">Settings</NavLink>
            </>
          )}
        </nav>
        <div className="account">
          <span>
            {user.fullName} ({user.role})
          </span>
          <NavLink to="/change-password">Change password</NavLink>
          <button
            type="button"
            onClick={() => logout.mutate()}
            disabled={logout.isPending}
          >
            Sign out
          </button>
        </div>
      </header>
      <Outlet />
    </>
  );
}

E. Hide what a role can't use

Two page files are replaced whole. The others get small edits. In each edit, the top text is what to find and the bottom text is what to replace it with. Whenever a useAuth import is needed, add import { useAuth } from '../auth/AuthContext.jsx'; with the other imports at the top of that file.

Replace all of src/pages/StudentsPage.jsx with:

jsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import { useAuth } from '../auth/AuthContext.jsx';
import StudentForm from '../components/StudentForm.jsx';
import StudentsTable from '../components/StudentsTable.jsx';
import { useDebouncedValue } from '../lib/useDebouncedValue.js';

function StudentsPage() {
  const { can } = useAuth();
  const [searchText, setSearchText] = useState('');
  const [page, setPage] = useState(1);
  const search = useDebouncedValue(searchText.trim(), 300);

  function handleSearchChange(event) {
    setSearchText(event.target.value);
    setPage(1);
  }

  return (
    <main>
      <h1>Students</h1>
      {can('staff') && (
        <section>
          <h2>Add a student</h2>
          <StudentForm />
        </section>
      )}
      <section>
        <h2>All students</h2>
        <input
          type="search"
          className="search"
          value={searchText}
          onChange={handleSearchChange}
          placeholder="Search by name or code"
          aria-label="Search students"
        />
        <StudentsTable search={search} page={page} onPageChange={setPage} />
      </section>
    </main>
  );
}

export default StudentsPage;

Run and test
Start the server and client, then:

Open http://localhost:5173. You're sent to /login.
Enter a wrong password. You see a red "Wrong username or password." Avoid many wrong attempts, since the limiter blocks you after 10.
Sign in as admin. You land on Students, and the top right shows your name and role, with "Change password" and "Sign out". Refresh the page, and you're still signed in.
Click Sign out. You're back at the login page. Press the browser's Back button, and it sends you to login again instead of showing student data.
Sign in as viewer1. There's no Evaluate link. Students has no "Add a student" section and no Actions column. Parameters and Settings show the data but no forms or buttons. Type http://localhost:5173/evaluate yourself and you see the "do not have permission" message.
Sign in as staff1. Evaluate appears. On Students you can add and rename, but there's no Delete button. Parameters and Settings stay read-only, and the "Parameters for this period" panel is hidden on Evaluate.
As admin, everything is available.
Forced password change. Create a new account (run this in the server folder):
npm run create-user -- temp1 "Temp User" staff
Sign in with the temporary password it prints. You're sent straight to Change password, and the menu is hidden. Try a short password, then two that don't match (both give red messages), then a good one. You land on Students. Sign out and sign in with the new password.
9. Ended session. Sign in as any user, then in a terminal run:

psql -h localhost -U stu_manager -d stu_database -c "DELETE FROM user_sessions;"
Click any menu link in the browser. You're sent back to the login page.
10. Deactivated account. Sign in as staff1, then run:

psql -h localhost -U stu_manager -d stu_database -c "UPDATE users SET is_active = false WHERE username = 'staff1';"
Click a link, and you're signed out. Sign-in as staff1 now fails. Re-enable with ... SET is_active = true ....

If something goes wrong

A blank page and a console error mentioning useAuth: AuthProvider isn't wrapped around App in main.jsx, or the useAuth import path is wrong.
Stuck on "Loading...": the server isn't running, or /api/auth/me fails. Check F12, then the Network tab.
Signed in but bounced back to login every click: the cookie isn't being kept, so check you're using http://localhost:5173 and not another address.
Buttons still show for a viewer: one of the small edits didn't apply, so check that file for can(.
